# Rashi on II Samuel

רש"י על שמואל ב

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel)

הפירוש הנפוץ והמוכר ביותר לתורה, המבאר את פשטי המקראות בתוספת הרחבות פרשניות; חובר במאה ה-11.

Most widely-read biblical commentary, compiled in the 11th-century, explaining the simple meaning of the text with interpretive elaborations.

Sections: Chapter-Verse-Comment

Location: Tanakh/Rishonim on Tanakh/Rashi/Prophets

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): Sefaria vocalized edition (Segments: 473, License: unknown)

Version (En): The Metsudah Tanach series, Lakewood, N.J (Segments: 473)


## Chapter 1


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:2:1)


**וְהִנֵּה אִישׁ בָּא מִן הַמַּחֲנֶה.** יֵשׁ בַּפְּסִקְתָּא שֶׁזֶּה דּוֹאֵג וְאֵינוֹ מְיֻשָּׁב עַל לִבִּי:


**Behold a man came from the camp.** The Pesikta¹ brings an opinion that this man is Doeig,² but the explanation is indefensible in my heart.³

footnotes:
¹ Pesikta Rabosi 12,9.
² Doeig HaEdomi is first mentioned in Shmuel 1,21:8.
³ It is clear from Shmuel 1, 22:18 that Shaul knew Doeig. Therefore, if in fact this man is Doeig, why did Shaul have to ask later in v. 8, “Who are you?” (Seder Hadoros, p. 102).



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:9:1)


**אֲחָזַנִי הַשָּׁבָץ.** (תרגום:) ״אֲחָדַנִי רְתִיתָא״. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה (ראה ילקוט שמעוני רמז קמא): מִשּׁוּם עָוֹן הַכֹּהֲנִים שֶׁהָרַג, וְכָתוּב בָּהֶן (שמות כח:ד): ״כְּתֹנֶת תַּשְׁבֵּץ״:


**I am gripped with convulsions.** I am gripped with convulsions.⁴ The Midrash Aggadah⁵ explains that this⁶ occurred because of Shaul's sin of killing the Kohanim⁷ as it is written concerning them,⁸ "a checkered shirt."⁹

footnotes:
⁴ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.
⁵ Tanchuma Metzoro 2.
⁶ Shaul’s death in this manner was retribution for that sin.
⁷ Of the city of Nov, see Shmuel 1,22:18.
⁸ The Kohanim in general.
⁹ Shemot, 28:4 lists the כתנת תשבץ as one of the garments worn by the Kohanim. Both the word תַּשְׁבֵּץ there and the word הַשָּׁבָץ here share the same three letters, ש־ב־ץ.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:9:2)


**כִּי כָל עוֹד נַפְשִׁי בִּי.** מַהֵר וּמוֹתְתֵנִי, טוֹב לִי שֶׁתְּמוֹתְתֵנִי אַתָּה, וְאַל יַהַרְגוּנִי אֵלֶּה וְיִתְעַלְּלוּ בִי:


**While I am still alive.** Hurry and kill me. I prefer that you kill me and not they¹⁰ kill and torture me.¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰ The Pelishtim.
¹¹ Prior to my death.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:10:1)


**וְאֶצְעָדָה אֲשֶׁר עַל זְרֹעוֹ.** (תרגום:) ״וְטוֹטַפְתָּא דְעַל דְּרָעֵיהּ״:


**And the bracelet that was on his arm.** The tefilin that was on his arm.¹²

footnotes:
¹² This is Targum Yonasan’s translation. He refuses to explain it to literally mean bracelet because that would have been taken by the Pelishtim as part of the spoils of war (Meam Loez).



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:16:1)


**דָּמְךָ עַל רֹאשֶׁךָ.** (תרגום:) ״חוֹבַת קְטוֹלָךְ תְּהֵי בְרֵישָׁךְ״, אֵין עוֹנֶשׁ בְּמִיתָתְךָ אֶלָּא לְעַצְמְךָ:


**Your blood is on your own head.** The responsibility for your death rests on your own head.¹³ There is no one to punish for your death but yourself.

footnotes:
¹³ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:18:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לְלַמֵּד בְּנֵי יְהוּדָה קָשֶׁת.** וַיֹּאמֶר דָּוִד, מֵעַתָּה שֶׁנָּפְלוּ גִבּוֹרֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל, צְרִיכִין בְּנֵי יְהוּדָה לְלַמְּדָם מִלְחָמָה וְלִמְשׁוֹךְ בְּקֶשֶׁת:


**He said, "To teach the Bnei Yehudah archery.** David said, "Now that mighty among Yisroel have fallen, the Bnei Yehudah need to be taught how to make war and how to pull back a bow.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:18:2)


**הִנֵּה כְתוּבָה עַל סֵפֶר הַיָּשָׁר.** הֲלֹא הִיא כְּתוּבָה עַל סֵפֶר בְּרֵאשִׁית שֶׁהִיא סֵפֶר יְשָׁרִים, אַבְרָהָם יִצְחָק וְיַעֲקֹב, וְהֵיכָן רְמִיזָא, (בראשית מט:ח): ״יָדְךָ בְּעֹרֶף אוֹיְבֶיךָ״, אֵיזוֹ הִיא מִלְחָמָה שֶׁמְּכַוֵּן בָּהּ יָדוֹ כְּנֶגֶד פַּדַּחְתּוֹ שֶׁהוּא מוּל עָרְפּוֹ, הֱוֵי אוֹמֵר זֶה קֶשֶׁת:


**Behold it is written in the Seifer HaYoshor.** This is written in Seifer Bereishis, which is the Seifer of the righteous,¹⁴ Avraham, Yitzchok and Yaakov. And where [In Bereishis] is it hinted at? "Your hand will be on the neck of your enemies."¹⁵ What type of warfare is it where he places his hand next to his forehead,¹⁶ which is opposite his neck?¹⁷ One must say: this is archery.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Rashi is citing the opinion of Rav Yochonon in Avodah Zarah, 25a.
¹⁵ Bereishis 49:8.
¹⁶ When pulling back the string of his bow. Rashi in Avodah Zarah, ibid writes the archer places his hand next to his eyes. This is how they sight their targets.
¹⁷ This interpretation reads the verse in Bereishis differently: “your hand will be on your neck.” See Marsho, Avodah Zarah ibid.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:19:1)


**הַצְּבִי יִשְׂרָאֵל עַל בָּמוֹתֶיךָ חָלָל.** מַצְבָּן שֶׁל יִשְׂרָאֵל, עַל תֹּקֶף מָעֻזְּכֶם נְפַלְתֶּם חֲלָלִים, כֵּן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן:


**Pillar of Yisroel, on your heights [you have been rendered] a corpse.** Pillars of Yisroel, on the mightiest point of your stronghold you have fallen as corpses. This is Yonasan's translation.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:21:1)


**הָרֵי בַגִּלְבֹּעַ.** הֶהָרִים שֶׁבְּגִלְבֹּעַ:


**Mountains in Gilboa.** The mountains in Gilboa.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:21:2)


**וּשְׂדֵי תְרוּמוֹת.** (תרגום:) ״לָא תְּהֵי בְכוֹן עֲלָלָא כְּמֵיסַת דִּיעַבְּדוּן מִנֵּיהּ חַלְתָּא״:


**Nor fields [yielding enough for] baking offerings.** There will be no harvest among [the fields] that have the amount [that necessitates] making the baking offering.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ If one prepares the amount of omer’s worth of dough then one must take off a portion of the mixture and give it to a kohein, see Shelach 15,20. An omer is the equivalent of forty-three and one-fifth eggs by volume.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:21:3)


**כִּי שָׁם נִגְעַל מָגֵן גִּבּוֹרִים.** מָגִינֵּי עוֹר הָיוּ לָהֶם, וּכְשֶׁיּוֹצְאִים לַמִּלְחָמָה מוֹשְׁחִין אוֹתָן בְּשֶׁמֶן, כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּהֵא כְּלִי זַיִן הַמַּכֶּה עָלָיו מַחְלִיק, כְּמָה דְאַתְּ אָמַר (ישעיהו כא:ה): ״קוּמוּ הַשָּׂרִים מִשְׁחוּ מָגֵן״. וְכָאן כָּךְ הָיָה מְקוֹנֵן הַקִּינָה, שָׁם נִגְעַל מָגֵן גִּבּוֹרִים, פָּלַט שׁוּמְנוֹ וְלֹא נִדְבַּק בּוֹ, וְנַעֲשָׂה כְּאִלּוּ לֹא נִמְשַׁח בְּשֶׁמֶן. כָּל ׳הַגְעָלָה׳ לְשׁוֹן דָּבָר הַפּוֹלֵט מַה שֶּׁנּוֹתְנִין בּוֹ, כְּמוֹ (איוב כא:י): ״שׁוֹרוֹ עִבַּר וְלֹא יַגְעִיל״:


**Because there, the shield of soldiers spit out.** They had shields made of skins and when they went to war, they smeared them with oil so that a weapon hitting [the shield] should slip [off of it]. As it is said [in Scriptures] "Get up officers, smear [your] shield with oil. And here this is what the Lamentation means, "There, the shield of soldiers spit out," it spit out its oil and [the oil] did not stick to it and rendered [the shield] as if it wasn't smeared with oil. All [uses of the word] הַגְעָלָה is a language of rejecting what had been put into it as [we find] "His oxen will impregnate, [the seed] will not be rejected."



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:22:1)


**לֹא נָשׂוֹג אָחוֹר.** לֹא הָיָה רָגִיל לִהְיוֹת נָסוֹג אָחוֹר:


**Would not be pulled back.** Was not used to being pulled back.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁹ Without hitting Yohanasan’s targets, thereby spilling his enemies’ blood and cutting through their fat.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:23:1)


**מִנְּשָׁרִים קַלּוּ.** לַעֲשׂוֹת רְצוֹן בּוֹרְאָם:


**They were lighter then eagles.** To do the will of their Creator.²⁰

footnotes:
²⁰ Rashi is paraphrasing Pirkei Avos 5,23.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:24:1)


**הַמַּלְבִּשְׁכֶם שָׁנִי עִם עֲדָנִים.** (תרגום:) ״דַּהֲוָה מַלְבֵּישׁ לְכוֹן לְבוּשֵׁי צִבְעוֹנִין וּמוֹבִיל לְכוֹן תַּפְנוּקִין״:


**Who has clothed you in scarlet clothing together with other delights.** That clothed you in coloured garments and loaded you with delights.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:25:1)


**עַל בָּמוֹתֶיךָ חָלָל.** (תרגום:) ״עַל בֵּית תּוּקְפָּךְ אִיתְקְטַלְתָּא״:


**On your heights, you were slain.** On your strong point you have been killed.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Samuel 1:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/1:27:1)


**כְּלֵי מִלְחָמָה.** שָׁאוּל וִיהוֹנָתָן, שֶׁהָיוּ כְּלֵי זַיִן שֶׁל יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**The weapons of war.** [Referring to] Shaul and Yohanasan who were Yisroel's weapons.



## Chapter 2


### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 2:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:8:1)


**לָקַח אֶת אִישׁ בֹּשֶׁת.** מִקְרָא הָיָה דוֹרֵשׁ שֶׁעֲתִידִין שְׁנֵי מְלָכִים לַעֲמוֹד מִבִּנְיָמִן, שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא לְיַעֲקֹב (בראשית לה:יב) ״וּמְלָכִים מֵחֲלָצֶיךָ יֵצֵאוּ״, וּכְבָר נוֹלְדוּ כָּל בָּנָיו חוּץ מִבִּנְיָמִין:


**Took Ish Boshes.** He extrapolated from a verse that two kings were destined to come from Binyomin. Because the Holy One, blessed is He told Yakov, "Kings will come forth from you," and [by this time] all of his children had been born except for Binyomin.¹

footnotes:
¹ “Will come forth from you” implies that they were to come from someone yet unborn, and this could only be Binyomin. Avner understood this to mean that two kings will be descendant from Binyomin, Shaul and Ish Boshes (Rashi and Sifsei Chachomin, Breshis 35,11).



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Samuel 2:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:16:1)


**חֶלְקַת הַצּוּרִים.** (תרגום:) ״אַחְסָנַת קְטִילַיָּא״, עַל שֵׁם הַחֲרָבוֹת, כְּמָה דְאַתְּ אָמַר (תהלים פט:מד): ״אַף תָּשִׁיב צוּר חַרְבּוֹ״:


**The Plain of Swords.** Yonasan translates the plain of Killing on account of the swords just like you say [in Scriptures] (Tehilim 89, 44)² "You also turned back the sharpness of his sword."

footnotes:
² Rashi is showing how the word צוּר, sharpness, is used in reference to a sword.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Samuel 2:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:23:1)


**אֶל הַחֹמֶשׁ.** כְּמוֹ דֹפֶן חֲמִישִׁית, מְקוֹם שֶׁמָּרָה וְכָבֵד תְּלוּיִין שָׁם (סנהדרין מט א) וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: אֶל הַחֹמֶשׁ, ״בִּסְטַר יַרְכֵיהּ״:


**Into the fifth rib.** As [if to say] the fifth rib,³ the place where the bile and the liver are set. [But] Yonasan translated [this to mean] "on the side of his thigh,"

footnotes:
³ Counting from the bottom.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 2:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:23:2)


**וַיָּמָת תַּחְתָּיו.** (תרגום:) ״וּמִית בְּאַתְרֵיהּ״:


**And died in his place.** And died in his place.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Samuel 2:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:27:1)


**לוּלֵא דִבַּרְתָּ.** אִם דִּבַּרְתָּ כֵּן, ׳לוּלֵא׳ כְּמוֹ ׳לוּ׳ וְעוֹד יֵשׁ לְפָתְרוֹ כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ, לוּלֵא דִבַּרְתָּ מַה שֶּׁאָמַרְתָּ (לעיל פסוק יד): ״יָקוּמוּ נָא הַנְּעָרִים וִישַׂחֲקוּ״:


**If you had not spoken.** If only you had spoken this [way⁵ earlier] [The לא] is as [if it were written] לו⁶, meaning it. Another [way] to interpret it is as it's plain meaning⁷: if you had not spoken i.e. that which you said "Let the young soldiers get up and playfight."⁸

footnotes:
⁵ Your decrying of us fighting each other.
⁶ According to this first interpretation, the construct לוּלֵא denotes לוֹלוּ, “if only if,” i.e., if only you had spoken these reconcilitory words earlier.
⁷ This interpretation of לולא is the usual “if not for.”
⁸ Those words triggered this deadly battle.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Samuel 2:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:29:1)


**הַבִּתְרוֹן.** שֵׁם מָחוֹז:


**The Bisron.** The name of a region.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on II Samuel 2:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/2:30:1)


**תִּשְׁעָה עָשָׂר אִישׁ וַעֲשָׂהאֵל.** וַהֲלֹא עֲשָׂהאֵל בִּכְלַל עַבְדֵי דָוִד, וְלָמָּה יָצָא, שֶׁהָיָה שָׁקוּל כְּנֶגֶד כֻּלָּם, וְכֵן (יהושע ב:א): ״לְכוּ רְאוּ אֶת הָאָרֶץ וְאֶת יְרִיחוֹ״; וְכֵן (מלכים א יא:א): ״וְהַמֶּלֶךְ שְׁלֹמֹה אָהַב נָשִׁים נָכְרִיּוֹת רַבּוֹת וְאֶת בַּת פַּרְעֹה״:


**Nineteen men and Asoel.** But is not Asoel included in [the group called] "the servants of Dovid," so why is he [singled] out? [The reason is] he was worth all of them [together] and so too [a similar case], (Yehoshua 2,1), "Go see [and spy out] the land and Yericho"⁹ and so too [another similar case] "King Shlomo loved many foreign women and the daughter of Pharaoh."¹⁰ (Kings I, 11, 1 ).

footnotes:
⁹ Yericho was as strong as the entire land combined (see Rashi there).
¹⁰ Shlomo cherished Pharaoh’s daughter as much as he did the rest of them put together. (See Rashi ibid).



## Chapter 3


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:3:1)


**וּמִשְׁנֵהוּ כִלְאָב.** וּבְמָקוֹם אַחֵר הוּא קוֹרֵא אוֹתוֹ וּמִשְׁנֵהוּ דָּנִיאֵל, וְלָמָּה נִקְרָא שְׁמוֹ כִּלְאָב, אָמַר רַבִּי יִצְחָק: שֶׁהָיוּ לֵיצָנֵי הַדּוֹר אוֹמְרִים, מִנָּבָל הָיְתָה אֲבִיגַיִל מְעֻבֶּרֶת, נֶהְפַּךְ קְלַסְתֵּרוֹ וְנִדְמָה לְאָבִיו. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (ברכות ד א): שֶׁהָיָה מַכְלִים פְּנֵי מְפִיבֹשֶׁת בַּהֲלָכָה:


**His second [son] was Kilov.** [But] in another place¹ [Scriptures] calls him, "and his second [son] was Doniel".² So why is his called [here] by the name of Kilov? Rebbe Yitzchok said [to answer this question] : Because the generation's cynics were saying that Avigail was pregnant from Novol,³ [consequently Hashem caused] his facial features to change and resemble his father.⁴ And our Rabbis said⁵ that [this name of Kilov was due to the fact that] he embarrassed Mephiboshes through Halacha.⁶

footnotes:
¹ Divrei Hayomim I, 3,1.
² i.e., refers to the second son of Dovid not as Kilov but as Doniel, his true name.
³ That Doniel was the son of Novol and not Dovid.
⁴ His resemblance to Dovid earned him the name of כִלְאָב a play on כּוּלוֹ אָב, “completely his father.”
⁵ Berochos 4a.
⁶ Doniel’s superior knowledge became evident during the course of his halachic debates with Mipiboshes. According to this Derosho, the letters כל of כִלְאָב stand for מַכְלׅים.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:5:1)


**לְעֶגְלָה.** זוֹ מִיכַל, שֶׁהָיְתָה חֲבִיבָה עָלָיו, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר (שופטים יד:יח): ״לוּלֵא חֲרַשְׁתֶּם בְּעֶגְלָתִי״. וְהַכְּתִיב (שמואל ב ו:כג): ״וּלְמִיכַל בַּת שָׁאוּל לֹא הָיָה לָהּ וָלָד עַד יוֹם מוֹתָהּ״, עַד יוֹם מוֹתָהּ לֹא הָיָה לָהּ מֵאוֹתוֹ מַעֲשֶׂה וְאֵילַךְ, קוֹדֶם אוֹתוֹ מַעֲשֶׂה הֲוֵי לָהּ:


**By Egloh** This refers to Michal⁷ who was cherished by him. Similarly it says "If you had not plowed with my calf" (Shoftim 14, 18).⁸ [But] it is written, "And Michal, the daughter of Shaul had no child until the day of her death" (Below, 6, 23) she didn't have one until the day of her death⁹?¹⁰ From that incident¹¹ and on [she had no children], but prior to that incident she did have [children].

footnotes:
⁷ The daughter of Shaul.
⁸ עגלה, “calf” is used in reference to a cherished wife just as a calf is cherished by it’s owner (See Sanhedrin 21a).
⁹ At which time she died in childbirth (ibid).
¹⁰ That verse implies that Michal had no children up to the time of her death, but our verse states that Egloh, i.e. Michal gave birth to Yisrom.
¹¹ Dovid rejoiced excessively when the Ark was returned from captivity. Michal rebuked Dovid for what seemed to her to be behavior beneath his dignity. This was not the case and she was punished for the unwarranted shaming of Dovid. (ibid).



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:6:1)


**הָיָה מִתְחַזֵּק.** בְּכָל כֹּחַ עַל בֵּית שָׁאוּל לְהַעֲמִיד מַלְכוּתוֹ:


**Was strengthening.** With all his might over the house of Shaul, to establish its monarchy.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:8:1)


**הֲרֹאשׁ כֶּלֶב אָנֹכִי אֲשֶׁר לִיהוּדָה.** כְּלוּם אֲנִי חָשׁוּב אֲפִלּוּ כְּרֹאשׁ שׁוֹמֵר הַכְּלָבִים אֲשֶׁר לְדָוִד, אַךְ לְפִי הַנִּקּוּד, שֶׁהַטַּעַם תַּחַת ׳הֲרֹאשׁ׳, וְ׳כֶלֶב אָנֹכִי׳ מֻקָּף בְּמַקָּף, כָּךְ פֵּרוּשׁוֹ: הֲרֹאשׁ, וְכִי אֶחְפֹּץ לִהְיוֹת רֹאשׁ בְּבֵיתֶךָ, טוֹב לִי לִהְיוֹת כֶּלֶב וְהֶדְיוֹט בְּבֵית דָּוִד, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן:


**Am I the chief dog [watcher] for [the kingdom of] Yehudah?** Am I at least as important as the chief dog watcher for Dovid. However, according to the cantillation which has the accent under [the word] ראׁשׁ and [the word] כֶּלֶב אָנׂכׅי coralled [together],¹² then this is the explanation: The chief? Do I want to be a chief in your house? It is better for me to be a dog and an ordinary person in the house of Dovid. This is also Yonasan's translation.

footnotes:
¹² Rashi’s first explanation is assumming that the words ראש and כלב are connected. However, the cantillation indicates that they are separate. Instead, the words כלב אנכי belong together. This compels Rashi to offer an alternative explanation.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:8:2)


**הַיּוֹם אֶעֱשֶׂה חֶסֶד.** מֵעַתָּה נָאֶה לִי לַעֲשׂוֹת חֶסֶד עִם בֵּית שָׁאוּל וְעִם כָּל אוֹהֲבָיו, כַּאֲשֶׁר עָשִׂיתִי עַד הֵנָּה וְלֹא הִמְצֵיתִיךָ בְּיַד דָּוִד:


**From this day on should I [continue to] be kind.** From now on [is it] proper for me to be kind to the house of Shaul and with all those who love him as I have done until now and I have not delivered you into the hand of Dovid?



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:12:1)


**תַּחְתָּיו לֵאמֹר.** (תרגום:) ״מֵאַתְרֵיהּ לְמֵימַר מְקַיְּמְנָא בְּמָאן דַּעֲבַד אַרְעָא״:


**In his behalf saying.** From his place to say, We shall keep this [oath] by the One Who made the world.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:12:2)


**לְמִי אָרֶץ.** לְשׁוֹן שְׁבוּעָה, בְּמִי שֶׁהָאָרֶץ שֶׁלּוֹ. דָּבָר אַחֵר: תַּחְתָּיו, שְׁמוֹ הִזְכִּיר תְּחִלָּה בָּאִגֶּרֶת, וְאַחַר כָּךְ הִזְכִּיר שֵׁם דָּוִד, וּלְכָךְ נֶעֱנַשׁ, כָּתַב: מִמֶּנִּי אַבְנֵר שַׂר צְבָא יִשְׂרָאֵל, לְדָוִד מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל, שָׁלוֹם:


**By He Who [posseses] the land.** [This is] language [used for] an oath, By He Who [posseses] the land. Another interpretation: "**Below him,**" [Avner] mentioned his name first in the message letter and afterwords he mentioned Dovid's name and that is why he was punished. [This is what] he wrote: From me, Avner, the general of Yisroel to Dovid, the king of Yisroel, peace salutation.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:12:3)


**לְמִי אָרֶץ.** לְמִי שֶׁהַמַּלְכוּת הָגוּן לוֹ, אֲנִי שׁוֹלֵחַ לוֹ לֵאמֹר: **כָּרְתָה בְרִיתְךָ וְגוֹ׳**:


**To He who [deserves] the land.** To whom the kingdom is proper for him, I am sending to him "saying, Enact a convenant, etc."



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:13:1)


**לִפְנֵי הֲבִיאֲךָ.** לִפְנֵי רְאוֹתְךָ פָנַי, הֲבִיאֲךָ אֶת מִיכַל:


**You first bring.** Before you see my face, bring me Michal.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:15:1)


**מֵעִם אִישׁ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״מִלְּוַת בַּעְלָהּ״:


**From [her] man.** Yonasan translated "from the company of her husband."



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:16:1)


**הָלוֹךְ וּבָכֹה.** עַל מִצְוָה הַהוֹלֶכֶת מִמֶּנּוּ, שֶׁכָּל אוֹתָן הַשָּׁנִים נָעַץ חֶרֶב בֵּינוֹ לְבֵינָהּ בַּמִּטָּה, וְלֹא נִכְשַׁל בָּהּ:


**Following and crying.** Over the good deed [about to be] leaving him because all those years he thrust a sword into the bed [separating] between him and her and he did not stumble [into sinful intimacy] with her.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Shaul did not consider her married to Dovid because he considered Dovid’s Kiddushin having been done using a loan which is an invalid Kiddushin. However, in fact, Dovid had used regular material wealth as his means for Kiddushin which made Dovid halachically married to Michal. This meant that Paltiel Ben Loyish was living in the same house with another man’s wife. For all those years Paltiel made sure never to touch her and thus was spiritually elevated by subduing his lustful inclination. Now that she was leaving, he was saddenned to no longer have the opportunity for this type of spiritual greatness. (Sanhedrin, ibid).



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:17:1)


**וּדְבַר אַבְנֵר הָיָה.** קדֶם לָכֵן:


**And the word of Avner was [sent]** Before this [incident with Paltiel]



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:18:1)


**אָמַר אֶל דָּוִד.** עַל דָּוִד, וְאֵינוֹ זָז מִמַּשְׁמָעוּת אֶל:


**Said about Dovid.** About Dovid, and this is not [an instance of the usual] meaning of the word, אל, "to".



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:22:1)


**מֵהַגְּדוּד.** פָּשְׁטוּ בַּגְּדוּד, לִשְׁלוֹל עַל הָאוֹיֵב:


**From an attack.** They sprung, attacking to take spoils from the enemy.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:26:1)


**מִבּוֹר הַסִּרָה.** שֵׁם מָקוֹם וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (סנהדרין מט א): בּוֹר וְסִירָה גָּרְמוּ לוֹ לְאַבְנֵר שֶׁיֵּהָרֵג, עַל שֶׁלֹּא הֶחֱזִיק דִּבְרֵי דָוִד בְּצַפַּחַת הַמַּיִם אֲשֶׁר לָקַח מְרַאֲשׁוֹתָיו שֶׁל שָׁאוּל, וְגַם עַל כְּנַף הַמְּעִיל שֶׁל שָׁאוּל אָמַר שֶׁמָּא אֶחָד מִן הַסִּירִים נֶאֱחַז בּוֹ וּקְרָעוֹ:


**From Bor HaSiroh.** The name of a place [But] the Rabbis said: A well and a thorn caused Avner to be killed, by not grasping unto the deeds of Dovid, by [ignoring] the jug of water that [Dovid] took from [under] the head of Shaul¹⁴ and also [by ignoring] the corner of Shaul's garment¹⁵ [Avner dismissed this and] he said "Perhaps one of the thorns caught upon [the garment] and tore it.

footnotes:
¹⁴ This is reference to Shmuel I, 26,12 where Dovid was being pursued by Shaul and Avner. Dovid found them sleeping and hence vulnerable. Not wanting to harm them but wanting to show Shaul he had been there, Dovid took the spear and the jug of water that was underneath Shaul’s head.
¹⁵ This is a reference to Shmuel I, 24,5 where Shaul, in pursuit of Dovid, stopped temporarily inside the cave where Dovid was hiding with his men. Dovid did not kill him but surreptitiously caught off the corner of Shaul’s garment.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:27:1)


**אֶל תּוֹךְ הַשַּׁעַר.** לִפְנֵי סַנְהֶדְרִין, לְהִשָּׁפֵט עַל דַּם עֲשָׂהאֵל אָחִיו:


**Within the gate.** [Yoav brought Avner] before a sanhedrin to be judged¹⁶ for the [spilling of] blood of his brother, Asoel.

footnotes:
¹⁶ The word שַׁעַר often refers to judges who usually hold court at the gate of the city, cf. Devorim 22,15.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:27:2)


**בַּשֶּׁלִי.** בִּשְׁגָגָה, שֶׁלֹּא הֵבִין אַבְנֵר שֶׁבְּלִבּוֹ לְהָרְגוֹ, וְסָרֵס הַמִּקְרָא, וַיַּטֵּהוּ יוֹאָב בַּשֶּׁלִי, אֶל תּוֹךְ הַשַּׁעַר לְדַבֵּר אִתּוֹ:


**Deceptively.** In a mistake provoking [way] so that Avner would not realize that it is in [Yoav's] heart to kill him. Cut up the verse [to rearrange it's words as follows] "and Yoav steered him deceptively to go within the gate to talk to him."



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:29:1)


**יָחוּלוּ.** יָנוּחוּ, יָחוּלוּ דְּמֵי אַבְנֵר עַל מִקְרָא שֶׁלְּפָנָיו הוּא מוּסָב, שֶׁאָמַר ׳נָקִי אָנִי וּמַמְלַכְתִּי מִדְּמֵי אַבְנֵר׳, יָחוּלוּ דָּמָיו עַל רֹאשׁ יוֹאָב, ׳דְּמֵי׳ הַנִּזְכָּרִים בַּמִּקְרָא שֶׁלְּפָנָיו:


**[The guilt] should fall.** The [guilt of the] blood of Avner should rest, should fall. [This verse is] referring back to the preceding verse that stated, I and my kingdom are innocent from [the guilt of] the blood of Avner [The guilt of] his blood should fall on the head of Yoav. The blood [of Avner] that was mentioned in the previous verse.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:29:2)


**וּמַחֲזִיק בַּפֶּלֶךְ.** נִשְׁעָן עַל מַקְלוֹ מֵחֲמַת חוֹלִי הָרַגְלַיִם:


**[A lame person] who leans on a cane.** Who leans on his cane because of diseased feet.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on II Samuel 3:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/3:30:1)


**הָרְגוּ לְאַבְנֵר.** כְּמוֹ אֶת אַבְנֵר, וְדוֹמֶה לוֹ (ישעיהו לח:יד): ״עָשְׁקָה לִי עָרְבֵנִי״, עָשְׁקָה אוֹתִי; וְכֵן (במדבר יב:יג): ״רְפָא נָא לָהּ״, רְפָא אוֹתָהּ; וּכְמוֹהוּ (דברי הימים ב יז:ז): ״שָׁלַח לְשָׂרָיו לְבֶן חַיִל לְעוֹבַדְיָה וְגוֹ׳ לְלַמֵּד בְּעָרֵי יְהוּדָה״, וּפִתְרוֹנוֹ, שָׁלַח אֶת שָׂרָיו אֶת בֶּן חַיִל:


**Killed Avner.** [As if to say] Avner¹⁷ and similar to it [we find] "Snatch me, be my surety" (Yeshaiya 38, 14), Snatch me¹⁸ and also similarly (Bamidbar 12,13), "Please heal her" Heal her¹⁹ and similarly to this (Divrei Hayomin II, 17, 7) "He sent his officers, Ben Chayil, and Ovadiah… to teach in the cities of Yehudah and it's meaning is: He sent his officers, Ben Chayil.²⁰

footnotes:
¹⁷ Without the ל because it is the object of the verse’s verb “killed.” Whereas Avner with the prefix ל would usually mean “to Avner.”
¹⁸ Not “to me” despite the verse using the word לי.
¹⁹ Not “to her” despite the verse using the word לָהּ.
²⁰ Not “to his officers, to Ben Chayil” despite the verse using the prefix ל.

**הַכְּמוֹת נָבָל.** הַכְּמוֹת רָשָׁע בְּחֶרֶב:


**The death of someone wicked.** Like the death by sword of a wicked person.

**יָדֶיךָ לֹא אֲסוּרוֹת.** הָיוּ וְאֵיךְ נָפַל גִּבּוֹר כְּמוֹתְךָ לִפְנֵי בְּנֵי עַוְלָה:


**Your hands were not bound.**²¹ How could a mighty soldier such as you fall before treacherous men.

## Chapter 4


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Samuel 4:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:2:1)


**הָיוּ בֶן שָׁאוּל.** הָיוּ לְבֶן שָׁאוּל, כְּמוֹ (שמואל א יג:ח): ״לַמּוֹעֵד אֲשֶׁר שְׁמוּאֵל״, אֲשֶׁר לִשְׁמוּאֵל:


**Were for the son of Shaul** Were for the son of Shaul similar to (Shmuel I, 13, 8) "until the time of Shmuel" of Shmuel.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Samuel 4:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:3:1)


**וַיִּבְרְחוּ הַבְּאֵרֹתִים.** כְּשֶׁמֵּת שָׁאוּל, שֶׁעָזְבוּ בְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל הֶעָרִים שֶׁסְּבִיבוֹת פְּלִשְׁתִּים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמוּר לְמַעְלָה, אָז בָּרְחוּ הַבְּאֵרוֹתִים:


**And the Be'erosites fled** When Shaul died, the people of Yisroel abandoned the cities that surround the Peleshtim as is mentioned above.¹ It was then that the Be'erosites fled.

footnotes:
¹ Shmuel I, 31,7.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 4:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:4:1)


**וְלִיהוֹנָתָן בֶּן שָׁאוּל.** מוֹנֶה וְהוֹלֵךְ אֵיךְ נִשְׁבַּת מַלְכוּת מִבֵּית שָׁאוּל, הוּא וּבָנָיו נֶהֶרְגוּ, וְזֶה הַנִּשְׁאָר, הֲרָגוּהוּ עַל מִשְׁכָּבוֹ, וּבְנוֹ שֶׁל יְהוֹנָתָן נָפַל וַיִּפָּסֵחַ:


**Yohanoson, the son of Shaul had.** [The verses] continue to recount how the monarchy was stripped from the house of Shaul. He and his sons were killed and this one [Ish Boshes] was killed on his bed and the son of Yohanoson fell and became lame.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 4:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:4:2)


**נְכֵה רַגְלַיִם.** שְׁבוּר רַגְלַיִם:


**Broken feet** Broken feet.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 4:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:6:1)


**וְהִנֵּה בָּאוּ עַד תּוֹךְ הַבַּיִת.** עִם תַּגָּרִים לוֹקְחֵי חִטִּים:


**They came into the interior of the house.** Together with the traders, the wheat buyers.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Samuel 4:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/4:10:1)


**לְתִתִּי לוֹ בְּשׂוֹרָה.** אֲשֶׁר דִּמָּה לְשַׂמְּחֵנִי, לְמַעַן תִּתִּי לוֹ שְׂכַר בְּשׂוֹרָתוֹ:


**He thought I would reward him for his good tidings.** He thought he was making me happy in order that I should reward him for his good tidings.



## Chapter 5


### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:6:1)


**אֶל הַיְבוּסִי.** מְצוּדַת צִיּוֹן נִקְרְאָה יְבוּס, וּמִזַּרְעוֹ שֶׁל אֲבִימֶלֶךְ הָיוּ, וְהָיוּ לָהֶם שְׁנֵי צְלָמִים, אֶחָד עִוֵּר, וְאֶחָד פִּסֵּחַ, שֶׁנַּעֲשׂוּ עַל שֵׁם יִצְחָק וְיַעֲקֹב, וּבְפִיהֶם הַשְּׁבוּעָה שֶׁנִּשְׁבַּע אַבְרָהָם לַאֲבִימֶלֶךְ, וּלְכָךְ לֹא הוֹרִישׁוּם כְּשֶׁלָּכְדוּ אֶת יְרוּשָׁלַיִם, לֹא לָכְדוּ אֶת הַמְּצוּדָה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (יהושע טו:סג): ״וְאֶת הַיְבוּסִי יֹשֵׁב יְרוּשָׁלַיִם לֹא יָכְלוּ יוֹשְׁבֵי יְהוּדָה לְהוֹרִישָׁם״, וְתַנְיָא אָמַר רַבִּי יְהוֹשֻׁעַ בֶּן לֵוִי: יְכוֹלִין הָיוּ, אֶלָּא שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ רַשָּׁאִין (ספרי ראה יב יז):


**To the Yevusi.** The fortress of Tzion was called Yevus. [Its inhabitants] were from the descendants of Avimelech. They had two idols, one [depicting] a blind person and one [depicting] a lame person. They were made to represent Yitzhok and Yakov.¹ [Placed] in their mouths was the oath Avrohom swore to Avimelech,² This is why they were not displaced [from Tzion]. When Yerushalayim was conquored The tribe of Yehuda did not conquor the fortress [of Tzion], as the verse states, (Yehoshua, 15, 63). "However the Yevusim who lived in Yerushalyim, the descendants of Yehudah were unable to expel them. [About this verse] it is learned: Rebbe Yohoshua, the son of Levi said, They had the ability to [expel the Yevusim] but they were not permitted to.³

footnotes:
¹ Yitzchak was blind at the end of his life (Bereshis, 27,1). Yakov was lamed during the course of his struggle with the angel (Bereishis 32,33).
² Bereishis 21,23–24.
³ Because of Avrohom’s oath to Avimelech. This point is made in the Sifri, Re’ey, Piska 72. However, there it’s brought in the name of Rebbe Yehoshua, the son of Korcho.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:6:2)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לְדָוִד.** מִי שֶׁאָמַר:


**And he said to Dovid.** Whoever said it,⁴

footnotes:
⁴ The speaker is unidentified, but is obviously one of the Yevusim.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:6:3)


**הָעִוְרִים וְהַפִּסְחִים.** הֵם עֲבוֹדַת כּוֹכָבִים שֶׁלָּהֶם:


**[The idols depicting] the blind and the lame** These were their idols.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:1)


**כָּל מַכֵּה יְבוּסִי וְיִגַּע בַּצִּנּוֹר.** (תרגום:) ״כָּל דְיִקְטַל יְבוּסָאָה וְיִשְׁרֵי לְמִכְבַּשׁ כְּרַכָּא״:


**Whoever kills the Yevusi and touches [and destroys] the towers spire** Whoever kills the Yevusi and has the strength to conquor the fortress.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:2)


**וְאֶת הַפִּסְחִים.** וְיַכֶּה גַּם אֶת הַפִּסְחִים וְאֶת הָעִוְרִים שְׂנוּאֵי נֶפֶשׁ דָּוִד:


**And [the idols of] the lame.** And destroys [the idols of] the lame and the blind which were abhorrent to Dovid.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:3)


**עַל כֵּן יֹאמְרוּ.** עַל אֲשֶׁר אוֹמְרִים: **עִוֵּר וּפִסֵּחַ.** בְּעוֹד שֶׁהָעִוֵּר וְהַפִּסֵּחַ יִהְיֶה בְּכָאן:


**Upon which they are saying.** Upon which they are saying, "The blind one and the lame one," i.e., while the blind one and the lame one are here.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:8:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:4)


**לֹא יָבֹא וְגוֹ׳.** יָבֹא דָוִד הֵנָּה הֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר, שֶׁאָמַר **כָּל מַכֵּה יְבוּסִי וְיִגַּע בַּצִּנּוֹר,** וְלֹא פֵּרֵשׁ מַה יַּעֲשֶׂה לוֹ, וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (דברי הימים א יא:ו) פֵּרֵשׁ: ״כָּל מַכֵּה יְבוּסִי בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה יִהְיֶה לְרֹאשׁ וּלְשַׂר״; וְדוֹמֶה לָזֶה (בראשית ד:טו): ״לָכֵן כָּל הֹרֵג קַיִן״, וְלֹא פֵּרֵשׁ מַה יַּעֲשֶׂה לוֹ, אֲבָל מֵעַצְמוֹ הוּא נִשְׁמָע לְשׁוֹן גְּעָרָה וּנְזִיפָה:


**[Dovid] will not come** Dovid will not come here. This is a shortened verse [because] it stated, "Whoever kills the Yevusi and touches [and destroys] the tower's spire" but [the verse] does not explain what happens to the person [who reaches the spire]. However, in Divrei Hayomim I, 11, 6) it explains, "Whoever kills the Yevusi first will be made a chief and a prince." Similar to this [we find]. "Therefore, whoever kills Kayin" (Bereishis 4, 14) and [that verse] does not explain what happens to the person [who kills Kayin] but from its context, it's understood that the language conveys rebuke and anger.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ To whoever kills Kayin.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:8:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:8:5)


**וְיִגַּע בַּצִּנּוֹר.** לְשׁוֹן גֹּבַהּ הַמִּגְדָּל, כִּי שָׁם הָיוּ נְתוּנִים הַצְּלָמִים שֶׁלָּהֶם, וּבִימֵי דָוִד כְּבָר עָבְרוּ הַדּוֹרוֹת שֶׁהֻזְכְּרוּ בַּשְּׁבוּעָה:


**And touches [and destroys] the tower's spire.** [This word] denotes the spire of a tower because that's where their idols were placed. [During] the days of Dovid, the generations of the oath had already passed.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Avimelech asked Avrohom to swear “that you will not deal falsely with me, with my son or my grandson.” These three generations had already passed when Dovid attacked the Yevusim in the fortress of Tzion.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:9:1)


**מִן הַמִּלּוֹא וָבָיְתָה.** הֶקֵּף חוֹמָה נְמוּכָה, וּמְמַלְּאִין אוֹתָהּ עָפָר, וְגֹבַהּ הַתֵּל בָּאֶמְצַע, וּמְשַׁפֵּעַ וְהוֹלֵךְ לְכָל רוּחַ, הוּא קָרוּי מִלּוֹא, וְעָלָיו בָּנָה דָוִד בָּתִּים, וְאוֹתוֹ הַמִּלּוֹא הָיָה סָבִיב לַמְּצוּדָה:


**From it's low wall and inwards.** A surrounding low wall; they would fill it up with earth, the summit of the mound would be in it's middle, and it would slant in all directions, that is called a מלוא, and Dovid built houses on [the wall]. That low wall surrounded the fortress.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:12:1)


**וַיֵּדַע דָּוִד.** בִּרְאוֹתוֹ כָּל מַעֲשָׂיו מַצְלִיחִין, וּמַלְכֵי הַגּוֹיִם שׁוֹלְחִין לוֹ מִנְחָה:


**Dovid understood.** When he saw all his endeavors succeeding, and the idol worshipping kings sending him a present.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:18:1)


**וַיִּנָּטְשׁוּ.** וַיִּתְפַּשְּׁטוּ:


**Fanned out.** They dispersed.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:20:1)


**בְּבַעַל פְּרָצִים.** מִישׁוֹר פְּרָצִים:


**Into the plain of Perotzim.** The plain of Perotzim


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:20:2)


**כְּפֶרֶץ מָיִם.** כְּמַיִם הַפּוֹרְצִים גְּדוּדֵי הַשָּׂפָה:


**Like water bursting over [walls].** Like water bursting over the walls edge.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:21:1)


**וַיִּשָּׂאֵם דָּוִד.** (תרגום:) ״וְאוֹקִידִינוּן דָּוִד וְגַבְרוֹהִי״:


**And Dovid burnt them.** Dovid and his men burnt them.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:22:1)


**בְּעֵמֶק רְפָאִים.** אֵצֶל יְרוּשָׁלַיִם הִיא, בְּסֵפֶר יְהוֹשֻׁעַ (טו:ח):


**The valley of Rephaim.** It is next to Yerusholoyim [as can be seen] in the book of Yehoshua (15, 8).



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:23:1)


**מִמּוּל בְּכָאִים.** (תרגום:) ״מִקְּבֵל אִילָנַיָּא״:


**From opposite the trees.** From opposite the trees.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:24:1)


**אֶת קוֹל צְעָדָה בְּרָאשֵׁי הַבְּכָאִים.** הֵם מַלְאָכִים הַצּוֹעֲדִים בְּרָאשֵׁי הָאִילָנוֹת, אֲשֶׁר אֲנִי שׁוֹלֵחַ לְעֶזְרָתֶךָ:


**The sound of stepping on the tops of the trees.** These are angels stepping on the tops of the trees that I am sending to help you.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 5:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/5:24:2)


**אָז תֶּחֱרָץ.** תָּרִים קוֹל מִלְחָמָה וְאַבְחַת חֶרֶב, גלפי״ד בלע״ז, וְכֵן (שמות יא:ז): ״לֹא יֶחֱרַץ כֶּלֶב לְשׁוֹנוֹ״:


**Then shout [war whoops].** Lift up [and bring forth] the sound of war and the fear of the sword Galfer in Old French, and similarly [we find] "A dog will not sharpen its tongue [growl].



## Chapter 6


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:1:1)


**וַיֹּסֶף עוֹד דָּוִד.** לֶאֱסֹף:


**Dovid again gathered.** To gather.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:1:2)


**אֶת כָּל בָּחוּר בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל שְׁלֹשִׁים אָלֶף.** לְפִי שֶׁנֶּאֶסְפוּ עָלָיו כְּשֶׁהִמְלִיכוּהוּ בְּחֶבְרוֹן, וְזוֹ הָיְתָה אֲסִיפָה שְׁנִיָּה, לְכָךְ נֶאֱמַר וַיֹּסֶף עוֹד:


**All the chosen men of Yisroel, thirty thousand.** Because they gathered for him when they crowned him in Chevron and [therefore] this was a second gathering [for Dovid] [Scripture] herefore says, "[Dovid] again gathered."



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:2:1)


**מִבַּעֲלֵי יְהוּדָה.** הִיא קִרְיַת יְעָרִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בִּיהוֹשֻׁעַ (יהושע טו:ט): ״וְתָאַר הַגְּבוּל וְעָלָה בַּעֲלָה הִיא קִרְיַת יְעָרִים״, וְכֵן בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (דברי הימים א יג:ו): ״וַיַּעַל דָּוִד וְכָל יִשְׂרָאֵל בַּעֲלָתָה הִיא קִרְיַת יְעָרִים״. וּ׳בַעַל׳ לְשׁוֹן ׳מִישׁוֹר׳:


**From Baalei Yehuda.** This is [the city of] Kiryas Yearim as it is said in Yehoshua, "and the boundary circled" and went up "to Baaloh, which is Kiryas Yearim" (Yehoshua 15:9). This also [can be found] in Divrei Hayomim, "Dovid and all Yisroel went up to Baaloh, which is Kiryas Yearim" (Divrei Hayomim I, 13:6). [The word] Baal means a plain.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:2:2)


**וַיָּקָם וַיֵּלֶךְ דָּוִד וְגוֹ׳ מִבַּעֲלֵי יְהוּדָה.** שֶׁנֶּאֶסְפוּ שָׁם **לְהַעֲלוֹת מִשָּׁם אֶת הָאָרוֹן וְגוֹ׳**:


**Dovid arose and went from Baalei Yehuda.** That gathered there to bring up the Ark from there…


###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:2:3)


**אֲשֶׁר נִקְרָא שֵׁם שֵׁם.** אֲשֶׁר נִקְרָא לוֹ לָאָרוֹן שֵׁם, וּמַהוּ הַשֵּׁם, שֶׁהָיָה שֵׁם ה׳ צְבָאוֹת עָלָיו:


**Whose name is called, the name.** The Ark is called a name, and what is the name? The name of Adonoy of Hosts was upon it.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:3:1)


**אֶל עֲגָלָה חֲדָשָׁה.** טָעָה בְּדָבָר שֶׁאֲפִילוּ תִינוֹקוֹת שֶׁל בֵּית רַבָּן יוֹדְעִין אוֹתוֹ, ״כִּי עֲבֹדַת הַקֹּדֶשׁ עֲלֵיהֶם בַּכָּתֵף יִשָּׂאוּ״ (במדבר ז:ט), וּלְפִי שֶׁאָמַר (תהלים קיט:נד): ״זְמִירוֹת הָיוּ לִי חֻקֶּיךָ בְּבֵית מְגוּרָי״, נֶעֱנַשׁ לָבֹא לִידֵי כָּךְ, וּמֵת עֻזָּא עַל יָדוֹ, לְפִיכָךְ כְּשֶׁהֱבִיאוּ מִבֵּית עוֹבֵד, הֱבִיאוּ בַּכָּתֵף, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (דברי הימים א טו:יב-יג): ״וַיֹּאמֶר לָהֶם אַתֶּם רָאשֵׁי הָאָבוֹת לַלְוִיִּם הִתְקַדְּשׁוּ אַתֶּם וַאֲחֵיכֶם וְהַעֲלִיתֶם אֶת אֲרוֹן וְגוֹ׳. כִּי לְמִבָּרִאשׁוֹנָה לֹא אַתֶּם פָּרַץ ה׳ אֱלֹהֵינוּ בָּנוּ כִּי לֹא דְרַשְׁנוּהוּ כַּמִּשְׁפָּט״:


**On a new wagon.** [Dovid] made a mistake about something that even children at their Rebbe's school know "Because the sacred work was [incumbent] upon them, which they had to carry on their shoulders" (Bamidbar 7:9). And because [Dovid] said, "Your statutes were songs for me in the house of my dwelling,"¹ (Tehilim 119:54) he was punished to come to this,² and [to have] Uzoh die through his hand. Therefore³ when they brought [the Ark] from the house of Oveid they brought it by shoulder as it says (Divrei Hayomim I, 15:12), "And he said to them, 'You, the heads of households for the Levites, you and your brothers sanctify yourselves and bring up the Ark… because, the first time you did it not.⁴ Hashem, Elokeinu made a breach upon us because we did not consult with Him as was proper'."⁵

footnotes:
¹ Hashem said to Dovid, “The words of the Torah upon which it is written, ‘Blink your eyes [and be distracted] from it and it is gone’ (Mishlei 23:5), you are calling songs?” (Sotah 35a). A song is of only temporary interest and then forgotten (Maharsho, ibid).
² Making the mistake of transporting the Ark by wagon.
³ After learning from this mistake.
⁴ Transporting the Ark on your shoulders.
⁵ Consequently leading to our tragic mistake.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:4:1)


**עִם אֲרוֹן הָאֱלֹהִים.** הֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר, וְהֵם בָּאִים עִם אֲרוֹן הָאֱלֹהִים:


**With the Ark of Elokim.** This is a shortened verse, [it means to say] and they were coming with the Ark of Elokim.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:5:1)


**בְּכָל עֲצֵי בְרוֹשִׁים.** שֶׁמְּתַקְּנִים מֵהֶם כְּלֵי זֶמֶר:


**With all [instruments made of] cypress wood.** Musical instruments manufactured from [cypress wood].



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:6:1)


**כִּי שָׁמְטוּ הַבָּקָר.** כִּי שְׁמָטוּהוּ הַבָּקָר וְנִעְנְעוּהוּ, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״אֲרֵי מְרָגוּהִי תּוֹרַיָּא״, כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּתַרְגֵּם (דברים יט:ה): ״וְנִדְּחָה יָדוֹ בַגַּרְזֶן״, ״וְתִתְמְרִיג יְדֵיהּ בְּפַרְזְלָא״:


**Because the ox had [made] it slip.** Because the ox had [made] it slip and had shaken it And so to is Yonasan's translation, "because the ox had [made] it slip," like [Onkelus] translates [the verse], "and his hand slips on the axe" [as follows:] and his hand slips on the axe.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Substituting the hebrew word וְנִדְּחָה with the Amaraic translation, וְתִתְמְרִיג denoting slipping just like he uses the same Amaraic word in our verse, i.e., מְרָגוּהִי.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:7:1)


**עַל הַשַּׁל.** עַל הַשְּׁגָגָה, שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ לִדְרֹשׁ קַל וָחֹמֶר, נוֹשְׂאָיו נָשָׂא בַּיַּרְדֵּן, הוּא עַצְמוֹ לֹא כָּל שֶׁכֵּן:


**Because of his mistake.** Because of his mistake, he should have expounded a fortiori [as follows:] the Ark carried its own porters in the Yardein,⁷ isn't it all the more so [that it is able to carry] itself.

footnotes:
⁷ When Bnei Yisroel crossed the Yardein into the land of Yisroel as stated in Yehoshua 4:11 and explained in Sotah, ibid.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:10:1)


**עוֹבֵד אֱדוֹם.** לֵוִי הָיָה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (דברי הימים א טו:יח), ״וּמִן הַשּׁוֹעֲרִים הָיָה״ (שם):


**Oveid Edom.** He was a Levite as it is wriiten in Divrei Hayomim (I, 15:24) and he was one of the gatekeepers.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Of the Bais HaMikdosh.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:11:1)


**וַיְבָרֶךְ ה׳ וְגוֹ׳.** אִשְׁתּוֹ וּשְׁמוֹנֶה כַּלּוֹתֶיהָ יָלְדוּ שִׁשָּׁה בָּנִים בְּכֶרֶס אֶחָד, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (דברי הימים א כו:ה): ״פְעֻלְתַּי הַשְּׁמִינִי״, ״שִׁשִּׁים וּשְׁנַיִם לְעֹבֵד אֱדוֹם״:


**And Hashem blessed.** His wife and her eight daughters-in-law each gave birth to six children in one stomach⁹ as it is said, (Divrei Hayomim I, 26:5–8) "Poualti, the eigth¹⁰… sixty-two [belonging] to Oveid Edom.¹¹

footnotes:
⁹ Pregnancy.
¹⁰ Child of Oveid Edom.
¹¹ See Berachos 63b.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:14:1)


**מְכַרְכֵּר.** מְרַקֵּד:


**Danced.** Danced.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:14:2)


**אֵפוֹד בָּד.** מְעִיל בּוּץ, פורציינ״ט בלע״ז, וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: ״כַּרְדּוֹט״, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן ׳מְעִילִים׳ דְּתָמָר אֲחוֹת אַבְשָׁלוֹם (שמואל ב יג:יח), ״כַּרְדּוֹטִין״:


**Linen robe.** A linen robe, *Foursaint* in O.F. Yonasan translated, robe as Yonasan also translated "the robes of Tomor, the sister of Avsholom," robes (Further, 13:18)



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:19:1)


**וְאֶשְׁפָּר.** אֶחָד מִשִּׁשָּׁה בְּפַר (פסחים לו ב):


**A portion of meat.** One sixth of a cow.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:19:2)


**אֲשִׁישָׁה אַחַת.** גַּרְבָּא דְּחַמְרָא (שם):


**A jug [of wine].** A jug of wine.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:20:1)


**אַחַד הָרֵקִים.** (תרגום:) ״חַד מִן סְרִיקַיָּא״:


**One of the riff-raff.** [Targum Yonasan translates:] One of the riff-raff.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:22:1)


**וּנְקַלֹּתִי עוֹד.** לְפָנָיו, יוֹתֵר מִזֹּאת שֶׁהֲקִלּוֹתִי בְּעַצְמִי עַתָּה:


**[Had] I made light even more.** Before Him [even] more then this [that] I made light of myself now.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:22:2)


**עִמָּם אֶכָּבֵדָה.** אֵינָם מְבַזִּין אוֹתִי, אֶלָּא חָשׁוּב אֲנִי בְּעֵינֵיהֶם עַל זֹאת:


**I would be more honoured among them.** They do not ridicule me but I am important in their eyes becasue of this.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Samuel 6:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/6:23:1)


**לֹא הָיָה לָהּ יָלֶד.** מֵאוֹתוֹ הַיּוֹם וָהָלְאָה:


**Had no child** From that day on.¹²

footnotes:
¹² Prior to that day, she did have a child, Yisroam mentioned earlier (Shmuel II, 3:5), see Rashi there.



## Chapter 7


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:1:1)


**וַה׳ הֵנִיחַ לוֹ.** אָמַר, הֲרֵי נִתְקַיֵּם (דברים יב:י): ״וְהֵנִיחַ לָכֶם מִכָּל אֹיְבֵיכֶם וְגוֹ׳״, מַה כְּתִיב אַחֲרָיו, ״וְהָיָה הַמָּקוֹם אֲשֶׁר יִבְחַר וְגוֹ׳״, מֵעַתָּה עָלֵינוּ לִבְנוֹת בֵּית הַבְּחִירָה:


**And Hashem granted him rest.** [Dovid] said [The condition] has been fulfilled, "And He has granted you peace from all your enemies etc." (Devarim 12:10). What is written after [that verse]? "Let it be that the place that [Hashem, your Elokim] chooses etc." It is now [incumbent] upon us to build the Chosen House.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:4:1)


**וַיְהִי בַּלַּיְלָה הַהוּא.** אָמַר רַבִּי חֲנִינָא בַּר פָּפָּא: אָמַר לוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא לְנָתָן, הָאָדָם הַזֶּה שֶׁאֲנִי מְשַׁלֶּחֲךָ אֶצְלוֹ, מָהִיר הוּא, שֶׁמָּא יִשְׂכּוֹר פּוֹעֲלִים וְנִמְצֵאתִי מַפְסִידוֹ, מַהֵר וֶאֱמוֹר לוֹ, לֹא אַתָּה תִבְנֶה הַבַּיִת. רַבִּי סִימוֹן אוֹמֵר: הָאָדָם הַזֶּה שֶׁאֲנִי מְשַׁלֶּחֲךָ אֶצְלוֹ, נַדְרָן הוּא, כְּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (תהלים קלב:ב-ג): ״אֲשֶׁר נִשְׁבַּע לַה׳ נָדַר לַאֲבִיר יַעֲקֹב אִם אָבֹא בְּאֹהֶל בֵּיתִי״, שֶׁמָּא יֹאמַר אֵינִי אוֹכֵל וְאֵינִי שׁוֹתֶה עַד שֶׁאֶעֱשֶׂה כָּךְ, וְנִמְצֵאתִי מַפְסִידוֹ (ילקוט שמעוני רמז קמג):


**And it was on that night.** Rebbe Chanina, the son of Popoh said the Holy One blessed be He said to Nosson, "The man I am sending you to is a quick one, he may hire workers and it will turn out that I [cause] him loss.¹ Hurry and tell him, 'It is not you who will build the Temple'." Rebbe Simon says² "The man I am sending you to is a vow maker" as it is said,³ "Who has sworn to Adonoy, vowed to the Mighty One of Yakov Surely I will not enter the shelter of my house"⁴ (Tehilim 132 2–3) "he may say, 'I will not eat, I will not drink until I do this'⁵ and it will turn out that I [cause] him loss.⁶

footnotes:
¹ Because it is premature to build the Bein Hamikdash at this time.
² That Hashem said to Noson.
³ Dovid about himself.
⁴ Rashi explains in Tehilim, verse 5 that Dovid meant he will not go into his house until he finds out in which location the Beis Hamikdosh is slated to be built.
⁵ i.e. build the Beis Hamikdash.
⁶ Slightly different versions of the opinions of Rebbe Chanina and Rebbe Simon can be found in Yalkut Shimoni, Shmuel II, 143.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:6:1)


**בְּאֹהֶל וּבְמִשְׁכָּן.** מִשְׁכַּן שִׁילֹה לֹא הָיְתָה בּוֹ תִּקְרָה, אֶלָּא בַּיִת שֶׁל אֲבָנִים מִלְּמַטָּן, וִירִיעוֹת מִלְמַעְלָן:


**Within a tent and the Mishkan.** The Mishkan of Shiloh had no roof, it had walls made of stone underneath and curtains on top [as its covering].



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:7:1)


**הֲדָבָר דִּבַּרְתִּי.** לְשׁוֹן תְּמִיהָ הוּא, לְכָךְ הוּא נָקוּד ה״א חֲטַף פַּתַּ״ח, וְהַדָּלֶ״ת רָפֵי:


**Have I said a word.** This language [conveys] astonishment, that's why its punctuation has the Hei with a Kometz, and the Daled is soft [with a Dogesh].



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:8:1)


**מִן הַנָּוֶה.** דִּיר הָרוֹעִים, כְּמוֹ (צפניה ב:ו): ״נְוֹת כְּרוֹת רוֹעִים״:


**From the corral.** The corral [used by] sheperds as [can be found], "the dugout dwelling of sheperds." (Zfsaphania, 2:6).



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:9:1)


**כְּשֵׁם הַגְּדוֹלִים.** זֶהוּ שֶׁאוֹמְרִים מָגֵן דָּוִד:


**Like the name of great people.** That is what is said [about Dovid] the shield of Dovid.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ Pesachim 117b. The “great people” referred to here are Avrohom, Yitzhok and Yakov. Just as they are mentioned in the first blessing of the Amidah, so too Dovid’s name is used in the ending of one of the Haftorah’s concluding blessings. (Rashi, ibid).


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:9:2)


**וָאַכְרִיתָה אֶת כָּל אֹיְבֶיךָ.** וּלְכָךְ עָלָה עַל לִבְּךָ לִבְנוֹת הַבַּיִת, כְּמָה שֶׁכָּתוּב בַּתּוֹרָה:


**I will destroy all your enemies.** And that is why [the thought] came to your heart to build the House as it is written in the Torah.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Devarim 12:10. See Rashi on verse 1.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:10:1)


**וְשַׂמְתִּי מָקוֹם.** עוֹד אֲנִי חָפֵץ לְהַשְׁקִיט, שֶׁיְּהֵא שָׁקֵט וְשָׁלֵו אֶת עַמִּי בִּימֵי בְּנֶךָ:


**I will make a place.** I still want to quiet [things] that my people [should have] quiet and tranquility during the days of your son['s reign].



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:11:1)


**וּלְמִן הַיּוֹם אֲשֶׁר צִוִּיתִי.** מְחֻבָּר עַל הָעֶלְיוֹן, וְלֹא יוֹסִיפוּ לְעַנּוֹתוֹ כַּאֲשֶׁר בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה קֹדֶם הַשּׁוֹפְטִים, וְכַאֲשֶׁר עָשׂוּ מִימֵי הַשּׁוֹפְטִים עַד כָּאן:


**And from that day that I commanded.** It is connected to what preceded it, they will no longer afflict [the people] as before, i.e., before the [days of the] Judges. And as they [also] did from the days of the Judges until now.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:11:2)


**וַהֲנִיחֹתִי לְךָ.** יוֹתֵר וְיוֹתֵר, עַד שֶׁתָּנוּחַ מִכָּל אוֹיְבֶיךָ:


**I will give you respite.** More and more until you have respite from all your enemies.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:11:3)


**וְהִגִּיד לְךָ ה׳.** הַיּוֹם עַל יָדִי, **כִּי בַיִת יַעֲשֶׂה לְּךָ** לְהוֹשִׁיב בִּנְךָ עַל כִּסְאֲךָ, וְיִתְקַיֵּם לְךָ בֵּית הַמַּלְכוּת, וְהוּא יִבְנֶה הַבַּיִת:


**Adonoy is telling you.** Today, through me [Noson], that He will establish a monarchy for you to have your son [Shlomo] sit on your throne and he will secure the monarchy for you. And it is he [Shlomo] that will build the House



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:14:1)


**בְּשֵׁבֶט אֲנָשִׁים.** זֶה הֲדַד וְרָזוֹן בֶּן אֶלְיָדָע:


**With the rod of men.** This refers to Haddad and Rozon, the son of Elyodoh.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Both were enemies of Shlomo, they are mentioned in Kings I, 11:14 and 23 respectively.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:14:2)


**וּבְנִגְעֵי בְּנֵי אָדָם.** זֶה אַשְׁמְדַאי, שֶׁדָּחָהוּ מִמַּלְכוּתוֹ, וְהַשֵּׁדִים בְּנֵי אָדָם הָרִאשׁוֹן הֵם, שֶׁכָּל מֵאָה וּשְׁלֹשִׁים שָׁנָה שֶׁפֵּרַשׁ אָדָם מֵאִשְׁתּוֹ בְּמוֹת הֶבֶל, הָיוּ הָרוּחוֹת מִתְיַחֲמוֹת וְיוֹלְדוֹת הֵימֶנּוּ (ילקוט שמעוני רמז קמו):


**And with the afflictions of people.** This refers to Ashmedoy who dethroned him.¹⁰ The demons are the children of Odom Horishon¹¹ because the entire one hundred and thirty years that Odom [stayed] separated from his wife after Hevel's death,¹² the spirits would become heated [pregnant] and give birth from him.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Gittin 68b.
¹¹ See Yalkut Shimoni 146. The word אָדָם in the verse is taken as a reference to Odom Horishon.
¹² Rashi in Bereishis 4:25 says that the Odom’s separation was due to the fact that he had brought death, to the world. The Yalkut Shimoni does not mention why the separation occurred. In addition the Yalkut Shimoni tells as that it was Chava who separated herself from Odom.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:18:1)


**וַיֵּשֶׁב לִפְנֵי ה׳.** לִפְנֵי הָאָרוֹן:


**And sat before the Ark of Adonoy.** Before the Ark.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:18:2)


**עַד הֲלֹם.** שֶׁהִמְלַכְתַּנִי:


**Until this point.** That You have made me king.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:19:1)


**גַּם אֶל בֵּית עַבְדְּךָ.** לְהַמְלִיךְ אֶת בְּנִי אַחֲרַי:


**Also, about the house of your servant.** To have my son reign after me.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:19:2)


**וְזֹאת תּוֹרַת הָאָדָם.** בִּתְמִיהָ, רָאוּי לְהִתְבַּשֵּׂר כֵּן לְבָשָׂר וָדָם. דָּבָר אַחֵר: עָשִׂיתָ לִי כְּמוֹ שֶׁעָשִׂיתָ לְאָדָם הָרִאשׁוֹן, שֶׁהֶרְאֵיתָ לוֹ דּוֹרוֹת הָעֲתִידִים לָצֵאת מִמֶּנּוּ:


**Is this anouncent [fitting] for [mere] man?** A wonderment. Is it fitting to announce such to [mere] flesh and blood¹³ Another explanation: You have done for me as you did for Odom Horishon¹⁴ that you had shown him the future generations that were to come from him.

footnotes:
¹³ To be informed of the future.
¹⁴ According to this interpretation, the word אָדָם in the verse is a reference to אָדָם הָרִאשׁוֹן.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:20:1)


**וּמַה יּוֹסִיף דָּוִד עוֹד לְדַבֵּר אֵלֶיךָ.** מַה אֶשְׁאַל עוֹד:


**What more can Dovid say to You.** What more can I ask for?


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:20:2)


**אַתָּה יָדַעְתָּ אֶת עַבְדְּךָ.** נָתַתָּ לִי כָּל צְרָכַי, כְּמוֹ (משלי יב:י): ״יוֹדֵעַ צַדִּיק נֶפֶשׁ בְּהֶמְתּוֹ״:


**You know Your servant.** You have supplied me with all my needs as [it says] (Mishlei 12:10) "A righteous man knows his animal's being.¹⁵"

footnotes:
¹⁵ The verse refers to a righteous person but Rashi is drawing an analogy to Hashem’s knowing the needs of his servants.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:21:1)


**בַּעֲבוּר דְּבָרְךָ.** לְהָקִים מַה שֶּׁאָמַרְתָּ לִשְׁמוּאֵל, לְהַמְלִיכֵנִי:


**Because of Your word.** To fulfill that which You told Shmuel to make me king.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:21:2)


**וּכְלִבְּךָ.** רְצוֹנְךָ הוּא, וְלֹא שֶׁאֲנִי כְּדַאי:


**And due to [the will of] Your Heart.** It is Your will [that I become king] and not because I am worthy.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:21:3)


**לְהוֹדִיעַ אֶת עַבְדְּךָ.** הַבְּשׂוֹרָה שֶׁבִּשַּׂרְתַּנִי:


**Of which You have informed Your servant.** The announcement of which You have informed me.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ That my kingdom will be everlasting.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:23:1)


**אֲשֶׁר הָלְכוּ אֱלֹהִים.** מֹשֶׁה וְאַהֲרֹן, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (שמות ז:א): ״נְתַתִּיךָ אֱלֹהִים לְפַרְעֹה״, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״דְּאָזְלוּ שְׁלוּחִין מִן קֳדָם ה׳״:


**For whom leaders went.** Moshe and Aharon as it is said, (Shemos 7:1). "I have made you a judge over Pharaoh" and so too did Yonasan translate, "messengers went from before Hashem."


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:23:2)


**לִפְדּוֹת לוֹ לְעָם.** כָּךְ אָמְרוּ הַשְּׁלוּחִים לְיִשְׂרָאֵל: הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שְׁלָחָנוּ לִפְדּוֹת לוֹ לְעָם, וְלָשׂוּם לוֹ שֵׁם, וְלַעֲשׂוֹת לָכֶם הַגְּדֻלָּה:


**To redeem for Him a people.** This is what the messengers said to Yisroel, The Holy One, blessed is he sent us to redeem for Him a people, to put forth His Name and to make great [things happen] for you.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:23:3)


**וְנוֹרָאוֹת.** עָשִׂיתָ לְאַרְצְךָ לְאַחַר שֶׁיָּצְאוּ מִשָּׁם, וּמָה הֵם הַנּוֹרָאוֹת, **מִפְּנֵי עַמְּךָ,** לְגָרֵשׁ עַמִּים וֵאלֹהֵיהֶם, וּמִקְרָא זֶה חָסֵר ׳לְגָרֵשׁ׳, וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (דברי הימים א יז:כא) פֵּרְשׁוֹ: ״לְגָרֵשׁ מִפְּנֵי עַמְּךָ אֲשֶׁר פָּדִיתָ מִמִּצְרַיִם גּוֹיִם וֵאלֹהָיו״, וְאֵינוֹ חִסָּרוֹן, כִּי מִמַּשְׁמָע שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר מִפְּנֵי עַמְּךָ גּוֹיִם, אָנוּ שׁוֹמְעִין לְשׁוֹן טְרוּדִין וְגֵרוּשִׁין:


**Awesome things** You have done for your land after [Bnei Yisroel] left there [i.e., Egypt]. And what are the awesome things? From before Your people You expelled nations and their gods. This verse is missing [the word] "לגרש", [however] in Divrei Hayomim (I 17:21) it explains, "To expel from before Your people that You redeemed from Egypt, nations and their gods." [Our verse] isn't [really] missing [this word] because from the sense conveyed as it is said, "From before Your people, nations" we understand that it means pushed and driven out.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:28:1)


**אַתָּה הוּא הָאֱלֹהִים.** שַׁלִּיט, וְיֵשׁ בְּיָדְךָ יְכֹלֶת לְקַיֵּם:


**You are the Elokim.** The ruler. The ability to fulfill [this promise] is in Your Hand.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Samuel 7:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/7:29:1)


**הוֹאֵל.** רְצֵה:


**Desire.** Desire.



## Chapter 8


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:1:1)


**מֶתֶג הָאַמָּה.** וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (דברי הימים א יח:א) כְּתִיב: ״וַיִּקַּח דָּוִד אֶת גַּת מִיַּד פְּלִשְׁתִּים״, וְהִיא נִקְרֵאת מֶתֶג הָאַמָּה, עַל שֵׁם שֶׁהוּא מַקֵּל רוֹדֶה בְּכָל הַפְּלִשְׁתִּים, מֶטְרוֹפּוֹלִין שֶׁל מְלָכִים, שֶׁלֹּא מָצִינוּ בְּכָל סַרְנֵי פְּלִשְׁתִּים בְּעַזָּה וּבְאַשְׁדּוֹד וּבְעֶקְרוֹן וּבְאַשְׁקְלוֹן שֵׁם מַלְכוּת, אֶלָּא בְגַת מָצִינוּ, (שמואל א כא:יא): ״אָכִישׁ מֶלֶךְ גַּת״:


**Meseg Hoamoh.** In Divrei Hayomim (I, 18:1) it's written, "Dovid took Goss from the hand of the Pelishtim." [Goss] is called Meseg Hoamoh because she was the stick that dominated all the Pelishtim. It was the king's metropolis because we do not find [associated with] any of the Pelishtim rulers [not] with Azoh, Ashdod, Akron [nor] Ashkelon,¹ a royal title. Only in reference Goss do we find [a royal title], "Akish, the king of Goss."²

footnotes:
¹ This a reference to the Pelishtim rulers enumerated in Yehoshua 13:3. Rashi has left out the Avites, because he relies on Rav’s opinion in Chulin 60b that Avites were from Teimon and not from the Philistine people.
² Shmuel I, 20:11.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:1:2)


**מֶתֶג.** אגויילו״ן בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Meseg.** *Avillon* in O.F.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:1:3)


**הָאַמָּה.** הוּא הַמַּרְדֵּעַ שֶׁל עֵץ:


**Hoamoh.** This is the harness made of wood.³

footnotes:
³ מֶתֶג means the reins. These are references to the control this city had over the Pelishtim.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:2:1)


**וַיְמַדֵּד שְׁנֵי חֲבָלִים לְהָמִית.** לְפִי שֶׁהָרְגוּ אֶת אָבִיו וְאֶת אִמּוֹ וְאֶחָיו, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (שמואל א כב:ד): ״וַיַּנְחֵם אֶת פְּנֵי מֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב״, וְלֹא מָצִינוּ שֶׁיָּצְאוּ מִשָּׁם:


**Those measured with two ropes were killed.** [This vengeance was] because they killed his father, his mother and his brothers as it is said, "He led them before the king of Moav." (Shmuel I, 22:4) We do not find that they ever left from there.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Yalkut Shimoni, 147.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:3:1)


**בְּלֶכְתּוֹ.** כְּשֶׁהָלַךְ הֲדַדְעֶזֶר:


**As he went.** When Haddadezer was going


###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:3:2)


**לְהָשִׁיב יָדוֹ.** כְּתַרְגּוּמוֹ: ״לְאַשְׁנָאָה תְחוּמֵיהּ״, שֶׁכָּבַשׁ מִן הָאָרֶץ חוּץ לִגְבוּל אַרְצוֹ, וְהִרְחִיב אֶת גְּבוּלוֹ:


**To enlarge his border.** As [Yonasan] translates, "To widen his border," [Haddadezer] conquored land outside his countries border and widened his border.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:4:1)


**וַיְעַקֵּר דָּוִד אֶת כָּל הָרֶכֶב.** מִשּׁוּם (דברים יז:טז): ״לֹא יַרְבֶּה לּוֹ סוּסִים״:


**Dovid destroyed all the chariot [horses].** Because [of the verse], "He must not acquire an abundance of horses for himself"⁵

footnotes:
⁵ One of the prohibitions specific to the kings of Yisroel. (See Devorim 17:16)


###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:4:2)


**וַיּוֹתֵר מִמֶּנּוּ מֵאָה רֶכֶב.** שֶׁהָיוּ צְרִיכִין לוֹ לִכְדֵי מֶרְכַּבְתּוֹ, וְהָרֶכֶב אַרְבָּעָה סוּסִים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (דברי הימים ב א:יז): ״מֶרְכָּבָה בְּשֵׁשׁ מֵאוֹת כֶּסֶף וְסוּס בַּחֲמִשִּׁים וּמֵאָה״, כָּאן לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁהָרֶכֶב אַרְבָּעָה סוּסִים:


**[With] one hundred chariot [horses] remaining.** That he required for his riding entourage. [The word] chariot [refers to] four horses as it is said, (Divrei Hayomim II, 1:17) "Chariot for six hundred silver pieces and horses for one hundred and fifty." From here we learn that a chariot refers to four horses.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Given that a מֶרְכָּבָה fetched six hundred silver pieces, four times the one hundred and fifty cost of one horse.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:7:1)


**שִׁלְטֵי הַזָּהָב.** הֵם אַשְׁפָּתוֹת שֶׁנּוֹתְנִים בָּהֶם הַחִצִּים, כְּמָה דְאַתְּ אָמֵר (ירמיהו נא:יא): ״הָבֵרוּ הַחִצִּים מִלְאוּ הַשְּׁלָטִים״. כָּל הַפָּרָשִׁיּוֹת הַלָּלוּ סְמָכָן אֵצֶל פָּרָשָׁה שֶׁל בֵּית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ, לְפִי שֶׁמִּכָּל הַמִּלְחָמוֹת הַלָּלוּ, קִבֵּץ הֶקְדֵּשׁוֹת לְצָרְכֵי הַבַּיִת:


**The golden quivers.** They are the quivers in which the arrows are put as you say, (Yirmeyah 51:11). "clean out the arrows, till the quivers." All these chapters⁷ have been set near the chapter concerning the Beis Hamikdash⁸ because from all these wars [Dovid] gathered consecrated items for the needs of the House [i.e., Beis Hamikdash].

footnotes:
⁷ Concerning Dovid’s battles.
⁸ Dovid’s desire to build a Beis Hamikdash for Hashem in the previous chapter (7:2) and Hashem’s subsequent response.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:13:1)


**וַיַּעַשׂ דָּוִד שֵׁם.** שֶׁקָּבַר אֶת הַהֲרוּגִים שֶׁהָרַג בֶּאֱדוֹם, וְהוּא שֵׁם טוֹב לְיִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁקּוֹבְרִין אֶת אוֹיְבֵיהֶם, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר בְּמִלְחֶמֶת גּוֹג וּמָגוֹג (יחזקאל לט:יג): ״וְקָבְרוּ כָּל עַם הָאָרֶץ וְהָיָה לָהֶם לְשֵׁם״. וּמִנַּיִן שֶׁקְּבָרָן דָּוִד, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּסֵפֶר מְלָכִים (מלכים א יא:טו): ״וַיְהִי בִּהְיוֹת דָּוִד אֶת אֱדוֹם בַּעֲלוֹת יוֹאָב שַׂר הַצָּבָא לְקַבֵּר אֶת הַחֲלָלִים״:


**Dovid made a name [for himself].** He buried those of Edom whom he had killed and that is a good name for Yisroel that they bury their enemies. As it says in reference to the war of Gog and Mogog, "All the people of the land will bury and this will acquire for them a name." (Yechezkel 39:13) And how do we know that Dovid buried them? Because it says in the Book of kings (I, 11:15), "And it happened when Dovid was in Edom when Yoav, his army general went up to bury the corpses."


###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:13:2)


**שְׁמוֹנָה עָשָׂר אָלֶף.** וּבְסֵפֶר תְּהִלִּים (תהלים ס:ב) הוּא אוֹמֵר: ״שְׁנֵים עָשָׂר אָלֶף״, אֱמוֹר מֵעַתָּה, שְׁתֵּי מִלְחָמוֹת הָיוּ:


**Eighteen thousand.** [However] in the Book of Tehilim (60:2) it says, "twelve thousand" Hence we must say [in response] that there were two battles.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ With Edom. Tanchuma Devorim.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:14:1)


**נְצִיבִים.** פְּקִידִים לִגְבּוֹת מַס:


**Officers.** Officers to collect the tax.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:15:1)


**וַיְהִי דָוִד עֹשֶׂה מִשְׁפָּט וְגוֹ׳ וְיוֹאָב עַל הַצָּבָא.** דָּוִד גָּרַם לְיוֹאָב לִהְיוֹת מַצְלִיחַ עַל הַצָּבָא, לְפִי שֶׁעָשָׂה מִשְׁפָּט וּצְדָקָה, וְיוֹאָב גָּרַם לְדָוִד לַעֲשׂוֹת מִשְׁפָּט וּצְדָקָה, לְפִי שֶׁהוּא דָּן, וְיוֹאָב שׁוֹטֵר וְרוֹדֶה עַל יָדוֹ, וְעוֹד, מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁיּוֹאָב עָסַק בְּמִלְחָמוֹת, לֹא הָיָה דָוִד טָרוּד בָּהֶן, וְלִבּוֹ פָּתוּחַ לִשְׁפּוֹט צֶדֶק:


**Dovid rendered judgement… Yoav…was [commander] over the army.** Dovid caused Yoav to be successful [with his command] over the army because he rendered judgment and righteousness and Yoav caused Dovid to render judgment and righteousness because he [Dovid] judged and Yoav policed and enforced on the basis [of the judgment].¹⁰ Another [reason is that] because Yoav busied himself with the battles, Dovid was not preoccupied by them and his heart was [able to be] open to judge righteously.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Rashi is learning that the proximity of the verse discussing Dovid’s judgment of the people to the next verse discussing Yoav’s commanding of the army shows that these two phenomena were related. Other prominent leaders are being discussed in the subsequent verses but Yoav is mentioned first immediately after king Dovid.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:16:1)


**מַזְכִּיר.** מַזְכִּיר אֵיזֶה דִּין בָּא לְפָנָיו רִאשׁוֹן, לְפָסְקוֹ רִאשׁוֹן:


**Recorder [of halachic cases].** [He] announced which [case] was to come before him [Dovid] first for it to be ruled on first.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:18:1)


**וּבְנָיָהוּ בֶן יְהוֹיָדָע וְהַכְּרֵתִי וְהַפְּלֵתִי.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וּבְנָיָהוּ בַּר יְהוֹיָדָע מְמַנָּא עַל קַשָּׁתַיָּא וְעַל קַלָּעַיָּא״. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (ברכות ד א) כְּרֵתִי וּפְלֵתִי, אוּרִים וְתֻמִּים:


**Venoyohaa, the son of Yehoyodoh** [was in charge of] the archers and the sling shooters. Yonasan translated, "Venoyoha, the son of Yehoydoh was appointed [in charge] over the archers and over the sling shooters." [Alternatively] our Rabbis said [that the words] keraisi and plaisi refer to the Urim and Tumim.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Rashi explains in Shemos 28:30 that this is a reference to the inscription of the Explicit Name which was placed into the folds of the breastplate. Through the breastplate, they would communicate the answers of Hashem to questions the Kohein asked of Him, hence they would (מֵאִיר), light up and explain their words and וּמְתַּמֵּם) complete their words, i.e. make words come true. (Yoma 73b). This explanation of our Rabbis concerning the translation of keraisi and plaisi can be found in Berachos 4a.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 8:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/8:18:2)


**כֹּהֲנִים הָיוּ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״רַבְרְבִין הֲווֹ״:


**Were prominent [leaders].** [Yonasan translates] "They were prominent."



## Chapter 9


### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Samuel 9:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/9:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/9:9:1)


**נָתַתִּי לְבֶן אֲדֹנֶיךָ.** הַמֶּלֶךְ רַשַּׁאי לְהַעֲבִיר נַחֲלָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּמִשְׁפַּט הַמְּלוּכָה (שמואל א ח:יד): ״וְאֶת שְׂדוֹתֵיכֶם וְאֶת כַּרְמֵיכֶם וְגוֹ׳ יִקַּח וְנָתַן לַעֲבָדָיו״:
