[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:35:2)


**כִּי אֶעֱלֶה וגו׳.** הֲלֹא מְעַט הֵם, וּמָה הֲנָיָה יֵשׁ לִי בְּכִלְכּוּלְךָ:


**That I should go up.** There are only a few left, [therefore] what pleasure will I have from your providing for me.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:36:1)


**הַאֵדַע בֵּין טוֹב לְרָע.** בֵּין מַאֲכָל טוֹב לְמַאֲכָל רַע, וְאָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ ז״ל (שבת קנב א): שָׁטוּף בְּזִמָּה הָיָה, לְפִיכָךְ קָפְצָה עָלָיו זִקְנָה:


**Between something good or bad?** Between good food and bad food. Our Rabbis of blessed memory said, He overindulged in sexual relations causing old age to overtake him.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ Shabbos 152a. Otherwise even an eighty year old man would not have deteriorated to that extent.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:37:1)


**כִּמְעַט יַעֲבֹר עַבְדֶּךָ.** הַלְּוָיָה מוּעֶטֶת יַעֲבֹר עַבְדְּךָ לְלַוּוֹתְךָ, אַחַר שֶׁתַּעֲבֹר אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן:


**Your servant will just cross over.** You servant will accompany you a little further after you cross the Yardein.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:37:2)


**הַגְּמוּלָה הַזֹּאת.** שֶׁאֵינָהּ טוֹבָה לִי:


**This type of reward.** That is not good for me.



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:38:1)


**עַבְדְּךָ כִּמְהָם.** בְּנוֹ הָיָה:


**Your servant Kimhom.** It was his son.



### Verse 43

###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:43:1)


**כִּי קָרוֹב הַמֶּלֶךְ אֵלַי.** מִשִּׁבְטִי הוּא:


**Because the king is close to us.** He is from our tribe.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:43:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:43:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:43:2)


**אִם נִשֵּׂאת.** כְּמוֹ ״מַשְׂאֵת״ (בראשית מג:לד), פְּרָס מִבֵּית הַמֶּלֶךְ:


**Have any presents.** As we find, "A present".⁸ A present from the king's palace.

footnotes:
⁸ Bereishis 43:34.



### Verse 44

###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:1)


**עֶשֶׂר יָדוֹת לִי בַּמֶּלֶךְ.** שֶׁאָנוּ עֲשָׂרָה שְׁבָטִים:


**We have ten parts of the king.** Because we are altogether ten tribes.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:44:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:2)


**וְגַם בְּדָוִד.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהוּא קָרוֹב לָכֶם, אֲנִי מָשׁוּךְ בּוֹ יוֹתֵר מִמְּךָ, שֶׁאֲנִי עֶשֶׂר יָדוֹת:


**And also more right in Dovid.** Although he is a closer relative of yours nevertheless we have more ownership of him then you do because we have ten parts [of him].


###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:44:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:3)


**וּמַדּוּעַ הֱקִלֹּתַנִי.** לִהְיוֹת אַתָּה קוֹדֵם, וְכִי לֹא הָיָה דְבָרִי רִאשׁוֹן לְהָשִׁיב אֶת מַלְכִּי, וְקֹדֶם לָכֵן בָּא דְּבָרֵינוּ אֵלָיו לַהֲשִׁיבוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב לְמַעְלָה (פסוק יב): ״וּדְבַר כָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל בָּא אֶל הַמֶּלֶךְ״:


**Why have you made light of me?** That you should be first, was I not the first to request to have my king return? Prior to this our request came to him to have him return as it is written earlier,⁹ "while the messages of all Yisroel are coming to the king."


###### Rashi on II Samuel 19:44:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/19:44:4)


**וַיִּקֶשׁ דְּבַר אִישׁ יְהוּדָה.** לְשׁוֹן ״הִתְקוֹשְׁשׁוּ״ (צפניה ב:א), נִרְאֶה וְהֻשְׁוָה דְּבַר אִישׁ יְהוּדָה, שֶׁהֶרְאָם אִגֶּרֶת שֶׁשָּׁלַח לָהֶם דָּוִד, ״לָמָּה תִהְיוּ אַחֲרוֹנִים לְהָשִׁיב אֶת הַמֶּלֶךְ וְגוֹ׳״ (לעיל פסוק יב), כָּךְ מְפֹרָשׁ בָּאַגָּדָה. וְיֵשׁ לְפָרֵשׁ, ׳וַיִּקֶשׁ׳, לְשׁוֹן ׳קֹשִׁי וְחֹזֶק׳, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״וְתַקִּיף״:


**The words of men of Yehudah overpowered.** Language similar to "Improve".¹⁰ The words of the men of Yehudah were more logical and they prevailed because they showed [as evidence] the letter Dovid had sent them [saying] "Why should you be the last to request" that the king return etc." This is what is explained in Agadah. Some explain וַיִקֶשׁ to denote hard and strong and that is Yonoson's translation, "overpowered."

footnotes:
¹⁰ Tsaphania 2:1.



## Chapter 20


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:1:1)


**וְשָׁם נִקְרָא.** נִזְדַּמֵּן, לְשׁוֹן ״הִקְדִּישׁ קְרֻאָיו״ (צפניה א:ז); ״וַיִּקְרָא אַבְשָׁלוֹם לְכָל בְּנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ״ (שמואל ב יג:כג), לְשׁוֹן זִמּוּן:


**There, he invited.** Invited Language like that of "He invited his guests."¹ "Avsholom invited all the king's children."² Language denoting "inviting".

footnotes:
¹ Tsaphania 1:7.
² Shmuel II, 13:23.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:4:1)


**הַזְעֵק לִי אֶת אִישׁ יְהוּדָה.** שֶׁיִּהְיוּ כָאן עַד שְׁלֹשָׁה יָמִים:


**Rally the men of Yehudah.** That they should be here within three days.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:4:2)


**וְאַתָּה פֹּה עֲמֹד.** לְסוֹף שְׁלֹשֶׁת יָמִים הֱוֵי זָהִיר לִהְיוֹת עוֹמֵד כָּאן לְפָנַי, כִּי חָפֵץ הָיָה לִרְדֹּף אַחֲרֵי שֶׁבַע בֶּן בִּכְרִי:


**And then you be back here [to meet me].** After three days be sure to be back here before me because he [Dovid] wanted to chase after Shevah, the son of Bichri.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:5:1)


**וַיּוֹחַר מִן הַמּוֹעֵד.** אַשְׁכְּחִינְהוּ לְרַבָּנָן דְּפָתְחוּ בְּמַסֶּכְתָּא, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּסַנְהֶדְרִין (מט א):


**But was delayed past the appointed time.** He found Rabbis who had begun to learn a tractate in the Talmud, as is stated in Sanhedrin.³



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:6:1)


**אַתָּה קַח אֶת עַבְדֵי אֲדֹנֶיךָ.** אַחֲרֵי אֲשֶׁר אִחֵר עֲמָשָׂא שַׂר הַצָּבָא, וְלֹא הֵבִיא אֶת צְבָא יְהוּדָה, קַח אַתָּה מִן הַנִּמְצָאִים סְבִיבַי, וּרְדֹף אַחֲרָיו:


**Take your master's servants.** Now that Amosoh, the commanding officer was delayed and had not yet brought the soldiers of Yehudah, you take from the men in my entourage and chase after him.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:6:2)


**וְהִצִּיל עֵינֵינוּ.** וְהִצִּיל עַצְמוֹ מֵעֵינֵינוּ, וַהֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר:


**To escape to out of our sight.** And he will escape right before our eyes, this is an abbreviated verse.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Missing the word עַצְמוֹ.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:8:1)


**מְצֻמֶּדֶת עַל מָתְנָיו.** סְמוּכָה מְאֹד לְגוּפוֹ לְרֹחַב מָתְנָיו, שֶׁלֹּא כְּדֶרֶךְ חוֹגְרֵי חֶרֶב, כְּדֵי שֶׁתְּהֵא נוֹחָה לִיפּוֹל, וְלֹא יִצְטָרֵךְ לְהוֹצִיאָהּ מִתַּעְרָהּ:


**Close to his loins.** Very close to his body along the width of his loins, this was not the usual way of carrying a sword. He did so to make it easy for it to fall out so it would not be necessary to take it out of its sheath.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:8:2)


**וְהוּא יָצָא.** כְּנֶגֶד עֲמָשָׂא:


**He started out [towards Amosoh.].** Towards Amosoh.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:8:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:8:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:8:3)


**וַתִּפֹּל.** הַחֶרֶב מִתַּעְרָהּ, לְפִי שֶׁהָיְתָה חֲלָקָה וּלְטוּשָׁה, וְאֵינָהּ תְּלוּיָה לְצַד יְרֵכוֹ אֶלָּא לְרֹחַב מָתְנָיו, וּכְמְעַט שֶׁשָּׁח עַצְמוֹ לְצַד הָאָרֶץ, נָפְלָה מִתַּעְרָהּ וַאֲחָזָהּ בִּימִינוֹ, וְלֹא הִכִּיר עֲמָשָׂא בַּדָּבָר:


**And it fell out.** The sword [fell out] from its sheath because it was smooth and sharp and was not hanging on the side of his thigh but it was along the width of his loins. As soon as he [Yoav] leaned forward towards the ground it [the sword] fell out of its sheath and he grabbed it with his right hand without Amosoh noticing anything.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:12:1)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר רָאָה כָּל הַבָּא עָלָיו וְעָמָד.** כַּאֲשֶׁר רָאָה הָאִישׁ, שֶׁכָּל הַבָּא עָלָיו הָיָה עוֹמֵד:


**When he saw that all who passed by it remained there.** When the man saw that everyone who passed by it would remain there.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:13:1)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר הֹגָה.** כַּאֲשֶׁר הֻמְשַׁךְ, כְּמוֹ ״הָגוֹ סִיגִים מִכָּסֶף״ (משלי כה:ד); וּכְמוֹ ״הָגוֹ רָשָׁע לִפְנֵי מֶלֶךְ״ (משלי כה:ה):


**When it was withdrawn.** When it was removed as we find. "When the impurities are removed from silver",⁵ and as find "When the wicked are removed from before the king".⁶

footnotes:
⁵ Mishlei 25:4.
⁶ Ibid, 25:5.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:14:1)


**וַיַּעֲבֹר בְּכָל שִׁבְטֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** שֶׁבַע בֶּן בִּכְרִי, לְפַתּוֹתָם שֶׁיַּמְלִיכוּהוּ עֲלֵיהֶם:


**He [Shevah] went through all the tribes of Yisroel.** Shevah, the son of Bichri [went] to convince them to declare him king over them.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:14:2)


**וְכָל הַבֵּרִים.** לֹא יָדַעְתִּי מַהוּ:


**All the Beirim.** I do not know what it is.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:15:1)


**וַתַּעֲמֹד בַּחֵל.** (תרגום:) ״וְאַקִּיפוּ מַשְׁרְיָין״. וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִים, וַתַּעֲמֹד בַּחֵל, לְשׁוֹן (איכה ב:ח): ״חֵיל וְחוֹמָה״, הִפִּילוּ הַחוֹמָה הַחִיצוֹנָה, וְנִתְחַזְּקָה לַעֲמֹד עַל יְדֵי הַפְּנִימִית:


**And it [the city] held by virtue of its inner wall.** [Yonoson translates:] "They surrounded it strongly." Some interpret וַתַּעֲמדׁ בַּחֵל to have a meaning like "the inner wall and the outer wall".⁷ They had [already] cause the outer wall to fall and it [the city] fortified itself to hold fast by virtue of the inner wall.

footnotes:
⁷ Eichoh 2:8.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:17:1)


**הַאַתָּה יוֹאָב.** שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּךָ (שמואל ב כג:ח): ״יוֹשֵׁב בַּשֶּׁבֶת תַּחְכְּמֹנִי״:


**Are you Yoav?** That it is said of you "Who sits among the wise at their assembly."⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Further on 23:8.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:18:1)


**דַּבֵּר יְדַבְּרוּ בָרִאשׁוֹנָה לֵאמֹר.** הָיָה לְךָ לְדַבֵּר לְשָׁלוֹם בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה, אִם שָׁלוֹם יַעֲנוּךָ וְיִפְתְּחוּ לְךָ, לֹא הָיָה לְךָ לְהִלָּחֵם עֲלֵיהֶם:


**You should have talked [peace] first.** You should have talked peace first. If they had answered you that they wanted peace and had opened their city to you then you should not have gone to war against them.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:18:2)


**שָׁאוֹל יִשְׁאָלוּ בְּאָבֵל.** אִם שָׁאֲלוּ בְּנֵי חֵילְךָ בִּשְׁלוֹם הָעִיר הַזֹּאת, שֶׁשְּׁמָהּ אָבֵל:


**If you had asked [for peace] with Oveil.** If your soldiers had asked for peace with this city called Oveil.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:18:3)


**וְכֵן הֵתַמּוּ.** מִיָּד הָיוּ בְּנֵי הָעִיר מַשְׁלִימִים עִמָּכֶם:


**They would have completely [cooperated].** The people of the city would have immediately made peace with you.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:19:1)


**אָנֹכִי שְׁלֻמֵי אֱמוּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** אֲנִי מִבְּנֵי הָעִיר, שֶׁשְּׁלֵמִים וְנֶאֱמָנִים לְיִשְׂרָאֵל וְלַמֶּלֶךְ וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה (בראשית רבה צד ט, ובילקוט שמעוני כא): סֶרַח בַּת אָשֵׁר הָיְתָה, אֲנִי הִשְׁלַמְתִּי נֶאֱמָן לְנֶאֱמָן, עַל יָדִי נִגְלָה אֲרוֹנוֹ שֶׁל יוֹסֵף לְמֹשֶׁה, אֲנִי הִגַּדְתִּי לְיַעֲקֹב כִּי יוֹסֵף חַי:


**I am [from the people of the city] that are loyal and trustworthy to Yisroel.** I am from the people of the city that are loyal and trustworthy to Yisroel and to the king. An Aggadic Midrash [states:] this was Serach, the daughter of Asher. I faithfully rewarded those who faithful [to God]: Through me, the location of Yosef's coffin was revealed to Moshe. I told Yakov that Yosef was alive.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:20:1)


**חָלִילָה חָלִילָה לִי.** חָלִילָה לִי, חָלִילָה לַמֶּלֶךְ:


**Far be it, far be it from me.** Far be it from me, far be it from the king.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:21:1)


**נָשָׂא יָדוֹ בַּמֶּלֶךְ בְּדָוִד.** בַּמֶּלֶךְ אֲפִלּוּ אֵינוֹ דָוִד, חַיָּב מִיתָה, וּבְדָוִד אֲפִלּוּ אֵינוֹ מֶלֶךְ, חַיָּב מִיתָה, וְכָל שֶׁכֵּן בַּמֶּלֶךְ בְּדָוִד, כָּךְ נִדְרַשׁ בְּמִדְרַשׁ קֹהֶלֶת (ט יח):


**Lifted his hand against the king, Dovid.** Against the king even if it were not Dovid he is punishable by death. Alternatively if it were against Dovid [who is a scholar] even if he was not the king, he [Shevah] is punishable by death. All the much more so [now that he has rebelled] against the king, against Dovid. This is how this verse is expounded upon in the Midrash Koheles.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:22:1)


**וַתָּבֹא הָאִשָּׁה אֶל כָּל הָעָם בְּחָכְמָתָהּ.** שָׁנִינוּ בְּתוֹסֶפְתָּא (תרומות סוף פרק ז): אָמְרָה לָהֶם: הוֹאִיל וְהוּא נֶהֱרָג וְהֵם נֶהֱרָגִין, תְּנוּהוּ לָהֶם, אִם הָיָה יָכוֹל לְהִנָּצֵל, כְּגוֹן שֶׁהוּא מִבִּפְנִים וְאַתֶּם מִבַּחוּץ, וְאַתֶּם בְּסַכָּנָה וְהוּא יָכוֹל לְהִמָּלֵט, אֵין דּוֹחִין נֶפֶשׁ מִפְּנֵי נֶפֶשׁ לְהָרְגוֹ בִּשְׁבִיל הַצָּלַתְכֶם, אֲבָל עַכְשָׁו שֶׁגַּם הוּא יֵהָרֵג עִמָּכֶם, הוֹאִיל וְנֶהֶרְסוּ הַחוֹמוֹת וְאֵין כֹּחַ לְהִמָּלֵט, מוּטָב יָמוּת לְבַדּוֹ וְאַל תָּמוּתוּ עִמּוֹ רַבִּי שִׁמְעוֹן אוֹמֵר, כָּךְ אָמְרָה לָהֶם: הַמּוֹרֵד בְּמַלְכוּת בֵּית דָּוִד חַיָּב מִיתָה:


**The woman came [and spoke] to all the people.** It is taught in a Tosefta:⁹ "She said to them, 'Since both he will be killed and they will be killed [they should] hand him over to them.¹⁰ If he were able to be saved, for example he was inside and you were on the outside and you were in mortal danger [fighting Yoav] [but] he could escape¹¹ [then we have a rule that] it is prohibited to let one person be killed in order to save another, [therefore, you cannot] have him killed to save yourselves. But now that he is [anyway] going to be killed with you because the walls have been breached and there is no possibility of escape,¹² [then] it is better that he alone die and you should not die along with him.' Rebbe Shimon said, this is what she said to them, 'Anyone who has rebelled against the kingdom of the House of Dovid is punishable by death.'¹³

footnotes:
⁹ Terumos 7:23.
¹⁰ To Yoav’s men.
¹¹ While you would be fighting.
¹² For anyone.
¹³ Threfore Shevah must be killed in order for you to be spared.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:23:1)


**וְיוֹאָב אֶל כָּל הַצָּבָא.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאָמַר דָּוִד עַל עֲמָשָׂא (שמואל ב יט:יד): ״כִּי שַׂר צָבָא אֲשִׂימְךָ תַּחַת יוֹאָב״, עַכְשָׁו שֶׁמֵּת עֲמָשָׂא, לֹא זָז יוֹאָב מִמְּקוֹמוֹ, וְעַל יְדֵי יוֹאָב הֻזְקַק לִמְנוֹת אֶת חֲבֵרָיו:


**Yoav commanded the entire army.** Despite the fact that Dovid had said to Amosoh, "I will make you the commanding general in Yoav's stead",¹⁴ now that Amsoh was dead Yoav was not replaced. And because of Yoav [remaining commander], he [Dovid] had to appoint his [Yoav's] friends.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁴ Earlier in 19:14.
¹⁵ To the positions mentioned in the next few verses.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:26:1)


**עִירָא הַיָּאִרִי.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״עִירָא יָאִירָאָה דְּמִן תְּקוֹעַ״. וְכֵן הוּא קָרוּי בְּסֵפֶר הַזֶּה (שמואל ב כג:כו): ״עִירָא בֶן עִקֵּשׁ הַתְּקוֹעִי״. וְ׳יָאִירִי׳ נִקְרָא, עַל שֵׁם שֶׁשָּׁנִינוּ בִּמְנָחוֹת (פה ב): תְּקוֹעַ אַלְפָא לְשֶׁמֶן, שֶׁשֶּׁמֶן זַיִת מָצוּי לְשָׁם, שֶׁמִּמֶּנּוּ מַדְלִיקִין הַנֵּרוֹת:


**Eiroh the Yoiri.** Yonoson translated: "Eiroh who gives forth light from Tekuah." And so is he referred to in this book Eiroh ben Eikash of Tekuah. He was called Yoiri because it is taught in Menochos,¹⁶ "Tekuah was first in [olive] oil," because olive oil was prevalent there from which candles are lit [in the Beis Mikdosh].

footnotes:
¹⁶ Menochos 85b.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 20:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/20:26:2)


**הָיָה כֹהֵן לְדָוִד.** לוֹ הָיָה נוֹתֵן כָּל מַתְּנוֹת כְּהֻנָּה שֶׁלּוֹ, כָּךְ אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ (עירובין סג א), וּפְשׁוּטוֹ שֶׁל מִקְרָא, שֶׁעֲשָׂאוֹ שַׂר וְשׁוֹפֵט:


**Was a Kokein for Dovid.** He [Dovid] would give him [Eiroh] all his Kehunah gifts, that is what our Rabbis explained.¹⁷ The simple explanation of the verse is that he [Dovid] made him [Eiroh] an officer and a judge.

footnotes:
¹⁷ Eiruvin 63a.



## Chapter 21


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:1:1)


**אֶל שָׁאוּל.** עַל עֲוֹן שָׁאוּל שֶׁנִּקְבַּר פִּתְאוֹם בְּהֶחְבֵּא, שֶׁגְּנָבוּהוּ אַנְשֵׁי יָבֵשׁ גִּלְעָד וּקְבָרוּהוּ, וְלֹא נִסְפַּד לְפִי כְּבוֹדוֹ:


**On account of Shaul.** On account of the sin regarding Shaul who was buried immediately and secretly as the men of Yovish Gilod stole his body and buried him. [Consequently] he was not eulogized with the respect due him.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:1:2)


**וְאֶל בֵּית הַדָּמִים.** שֶׁהָרַג נוֹב עִיר הַכֹּהֲנִים:


**And his bloody House.** Because he killed out Nov, the city of Kohanim.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:1:3)


**עַל אֲשֶׁר הֵמִית אֶת הַגִּבְעֹנִים.** כְּשֶׁהָרַג נוֹב עִיר הַכֹּהֲנִים, הֵמִית מֵהֶם שִׁבְעָה, שְׁנֵי חוֹטְבֵי עֵצִים, וּשְׁנֵי שׁוֹאֲבֵי מַיִם, וְשַׁמָּשׁ וְחַזָּן וְסוֹפֵר, כָּךְ מְפֹרָשׁ בְּגָמָרָא יְרוּשַׁלְמִית בְּסַנְהֶדְרִין (יז) וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ: מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁהָרַג אֶת הַכֹּהֲנִים שֶׁהָיוּ מַסְפִּיקִין לָהֶם מָזוֹן, הֶעֱלָה עָלָיו הַכָּתוּב כְּאִלּוּ הֱמִיתָם. וְאַל תִּתְמַהּ שֶׁהֲרֵי הָיָה הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא תּוֹבֵעַ כְּבוֹדוֹ וְתוֹבֵעַ סִרְחוֹנוֹ, שֶׁכָּךְ כָּתוּב (צפניה ב:ג): ״אֲשֶׁר מִשְׁפָּטוֹ פָּעָלוּ״ בַּאֲשֶׁר מִשְׁפָּטוֹ, שָׁם פָּעֳלוֹ, כָּךְ שָׁנִינוּ בִּיבָמוֹת (עח ב):


**And because he killed the Givonim.** When he killed out Nov, the city of Kohanim, he killed seven people from among them [the Givonim]. Two wood cutters, two water drawers, the sexton, the announcer and the scribe. This is what is explained in Yerushalmi Talmud, in Maseches Sanhedrin.¹ [But] our Rabbis said,² On account that he [Shaul] killed the Kohanim [of Nov] that provided food for them [the Givonim], consequently Scripture considers it as if he killed them [as well]. Do not wonder that God demanded retribution for the lack of respect given to him³ and simultaneously demanded retribution for his sin,⁴ because this what is what it is written, "Who has done His work,"⁵ when the person is being judged his good works [are remembered] this what we are taught in Maseches Yevomos.⁶

footnotes:
¹ Yerushalmi Sanhedrin 29a.
² Yevomos 78b.
³ i.e., the proper eulogy due him.
⁴ of killing out Nov.
⁵ Tsafanyoh 2:3.
⁶ Yevomos ibid, See Rashi there.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר אֲלֵיהֶם.** דִּבְרֵי רִצּוּי, שֶׁיַּעַבְרוּ עַל מִדּוֹתֵיהֶם וְיִמְחֲלוּ לְשָׁאוּל וּלְבֵיתוֹ:


**And spoke to them.** Words of appeasement [asking them] to put aside their feelings [of anger] and forgive Shaul and his household.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:2)


**וְהַגִּבְעֹנִים לֹא מִבְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל הֵמָּה.** כְּלוֹמַר וְהֵן הֶרְאוּ בְּעַצְמָם מִדַּת אַכְזָרִיּוּת, שֶׁאֵינָן מִזַּרְעוֹ שֶׁל אַבְרָהָם אָבִינוּ, וְאֵינָן רְאוּיִין לִדָּבֵק בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל, וּלְכָךְ גָּזַר עֲלֵיהֶם דָּוִד שֶׁלֹּא יָבוֹאוּ בַקָּהָל, אָמַר: שְׁלֹשָׁה סִימָנִים יֵשׁ בְּאוּמָּה זוֹ, רַחֲמָנִים וּבַיְשָׁנִים וְגוֹמְלֵי חֲסָדִים, מִי שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ שְׁלֹשָׁה סִימָנִים הַלָּלוּ, רָאוּי לִדָּבֵק בּוֹ:


**The Givonim were not part of the Bnei Yisroel.** This is to say that they displayed how cruel they are, because they are not descendants of our forefather Avrohom. They have proven themselves unworthy to join with Yisroel and hence Dovid decreed that they not be permitted to marry into the Jewish community. He [Dovid] said, This nation [of Yisroel] has three characteristics, the people are merciful, they possess shame and perform kindful deeds for others. Whoever has these three characteristics is worthy of joining it [Yisroel].⁷

footnotes:
⁷ They have shown themselves to be cruel and unmerciful and hence they do not deserve to join together with Yisroel. Yevomos 79a.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:3)


**נִשְׁבְּעוּ לָהֶם.** בִּימֵי יְהוֹשֻׁעַ כְּשֶׁנְּתָנוּם חוֹטְבֵי עֵצִים וְשׁוֹאֲבֵי מַיִם לַמִּזְבֵּחַ (יהושע ט:טו):


**Sworn to them [that they would let them live].** During the days of Yehoshua when he made woodcutters and water drawers for the Altar.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Yehoshua 9:27.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:2:4)


**בְּקַנְּאֹתוֹ לִבְנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** בְּתִתּוֹ לֵב לְנַקּוֹת וּלְטַהֵר אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל וְלַעֲשׂוֹת צָרְכֵיהֶם, בִּקֵּשׁ לְהָרְגָם, וְקִנְאָה זוֹ לְטוֹבָה, כְּמוֹ (במדבר יא:כט): ״הַמְקַנֵּא אַתָּה לִי״; וְכֵן (מלכים ב י:טז): ״בְּקִנְאָתִי לַה׳״ דְּיֵהוּא זֶהוּ פְשׁוּטוֹ. וּלְפִי מִדְרָשׁוֹ, לֹא אָמַר לַהֲרוֹג אֶלָּא אֶת הַכֹּהֲנִים, וְקִנְאָה זוֹ הָאֲמוּרָה כָּאן, אֵינָהּ לְטוֹבָה כִּי אִם לְרָעָה, שֶׁיָּדַע שֶׁלֹּא תִּמְשׁוֹךְ מַלְכוּתוֹ מִיּוֹם שֶׁלֹּא שָׁמַר מִצְוַת הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בַּעֲמָלֵק, וּמֵאָז נִתְקַנֵּא בָּהֶם:


**In his zeal to avenge the Bnei Yisroel.** Because he [Shaul] intended to clean and purify Yisroel and to take care of their [Yisroel's] needs, he wanted to kill them. This anger was for a good purpose as we find "Are you zealous for my sake?"⁹ And we also find "by the zeal for God's sake" as was said of Yeihuh.¹⁰ This is the simple explanation of our verse. [But] according to the Midrashic intepretation, he [Shaul] only ordered that the kohanim be killed. And the zeal described here was not for a good purpose but rather for a bad purpose because he [Shaul] knew that his kingdom would not continue further from the day that he did not heed God's commandment concerning Amoleik,¹¹ and from that time on, he was angry [and persecuted] them.

footnotes:
⁹ Bamidbar 11:29.
¹⁰ See Melochim I, 19:10.
¹¹ Shmuel I, 15:9.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:3:1)


**וּבָרְכוּ אֶת נַחֲלַת ה׳.** הִתְפַּלְּלוּ עֲלֵיהֶם:


**So that you should bless the heritage of Adonoy?** Pray for them.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:4:1)


**אֵין לָנוּ כֶּסֶף וְזָהָב עִם שָׁאוּל.** כִּי אִם נְפָשׁוֹת, בִּקֵּשׁ לְפַיְּסָם בְּמָמוֹן, וְלֹא רָצוּ, אָמַר שֶׁמָּא הֵם מִתְבַּיְּשִׁין זֶה מִזֶּה, הָלַךְ וּפִיֵּס כָּל אֶחָד וְאֶחָד לְעַצְמוֹ, וְלֹא נִתְפַּיְּסוּ, לְכָךְ כְּתִיב: אֵין לִי כֶּסֶף וְזָהָב וְגוֹ׳, לְשׁוֹן יָחִיד, זוֹ רָאִיתִי בִּירוּשַׁלְמִי (סנהדרין ו:ז):


**We are not interested in silver or gold from Shaul.** We are only interested in [retribution against] people. He [Dovid] wanted to appease them with money but they did not want [to accept it]. He [Dovid] said, "It may be that they are embarrassed of each other." [Therefore] he went and tried to appease each one individually [but] they [still] would not be appeased. That is why it is written here, "I am not interested in silver or gold etc" in singular form.¹² This I saw in the Yerushalmi Talmud.¹³

footnotes:
¹² The verse is written with לִי in singular form, but is read with לָנוּ, plural form indicating that Dovid tried to appease them both collectively as a group and also on an individual basis.
¹³ Sanhedrin 29a.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:4:2)


**וְאֵין לָנוּ אִישׁ לְהָמִית.** בְּכָל שְׁאָר בְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁלֹּא חָטְאוּ לָנוּ:


**And we are interested in killing anyone.** Among the rest of the Bnei Yisroel because they did not agrieve us.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:5:1)


**וַאֲשֶׁר דִּמָּה לָנוּ נִשְׁמַדְנוּ.** אֲשֶׁר אָמַר בְּלִבּוֹ, שֶׁנִּשְׁמַדְנוּ עַל יָדוֹ מֵהִתְיַצֵּב וְגוֹ׳, שֶׁחָשַׁב לְהַשְׁמִידֵנוּ:


**And intended to destroy us.** He said in his heart that we should be destroyed [and prevented] by him "from establishing etc." He intended to destroy us.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:5:2)


**דִּמָּה לָנוּ.** חָשַׁב עָלֵינוּ לְהַשְׁמִידֵנוּ:


**Intended.** He intended to destroy us.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:6:1)


**וְהוֹקַעֲנוּם.** תְּלִיָּה:


**We will hang them.** Hanging.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:6:2)


**לַה׳.** לִשְׁמוֹ שֶׁל מָקוֹם, לְהוֹדִיעַ מִשְׁפָּטוֹ:


**Before God.** In the name of God, to publicize His edict.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:6:3)


**בְּחִיר ה׳.** בַּת קוֹל יָצְאָה וְאָמְרָה: בְּחִיר ה׳ (ברכות יב ב):


**The chosen one of God.** A heavenly voice came forth and said, "The chosen one of God."¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ The Givonim who hated Shaul would be unlikely to call him, “the chosen one of God.”



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:7:1)


**וַיַּחְמֹל הַמֶּלֶךְ.** בִּקֵּשׁ רַחֲמִים שֶׁלֹּא יִקְלְטֶנּוּ הָאָרוֹן, שֶׁהֶעֱבִירָם לִפְנֵי הָאָרוֹן, כָּל שֶׁהָאָרוֹן קוֹלְטוֹ, לְמִיתָה (יבמות עט א):


**The king had pity.** He prayed for pity on Mephivoshes that the Ark should not detain him; because Dovid had them [Shaul's children] pass before the Ark, whomever the Ark detained, was put to death.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ i.e., was handed over to the Givonim who subsequently put them to death. Yevomos 79a.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:8:1)


**בְּנֵי מִיכַל בַּת שָׁאוּל אֲשֶׁר יָלְדָה לְעַדְרִיאֵל.** וְכִי מִיכַל יָלְדָה, וַהֲלֹא מֵירַב יָלְדָה לוֹ, אֶלָּא מֵירַב יָלְדָה, וּמִיכַל גִּדְּלָה, לְפִיכָךְ נִקְרָא עַל שְׁמָהּ, וְהַמְגַדֵּל יָתוֹם וִיתוֹמָה בְּתוֹךְ בֵּיתוֹ, כְּאִלּוּ יְלָדוֹ וְנִקְרָא עַל שְׁמוֹ (סנהדרין יט ב):


**Sons of Michal, the daughter of Shaul who had bore them to Adriel.** Did Michal give birth [to these children]? It was Meirav who bore [those children] to [Adriel]. [The explanation is] that Meirav gave birth [to them] and Michal¹⁶ raised them, that is why they are called by her name because someone who raises male and female orphans in their house it is considered as if that person gave birth [to the child] and [the child] is called by his name.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁶ Meirav’s sister.
¹⁷ Sanhedrin 19a.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:9:1)


**בִּתְחִלַּת קְצִיר.** בִּימֵי נִיסָן:


**At the beginning of the harvest.** During the days of Nisson.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:10:1)


**עַד נִתַּךְ מַיִם.** בִּימוֹת הַגְּשָׁמִים בְּתִשְׁרֵי, שֶׁלֹּא נִתְּנוּ לִקְבוּרָה וַהֲלֹא כְתִיב (דברים כא:כג): ״לֹא תָלִין נִבְלָתוֹ״, אֶלָּא אָמְרוּ מוּטָב תֵּעָקֵר אוֹת אַחַת מִן הַתּוֹרָה, וְיִתְקַדֵּשׁ שֵׁם שָׁמַיִם בְּפַרְהֶסְיָא, שֶׁהָיוּ עוֹבְרִין וְשָׁבִין אוֹמְרִים: מַה טִיבָן שֶׁל אֵלּוּ, אוֹמְרִים לָהֶם: מִבְּנֵי מְלָכִים הֵם, וּמֶה עָשׂוּ, פָּשְׁטוּ יָדָם בְּגֵרִים גְּרוּרִים, אוֹמְרִים: אֵין לְךָ אֻמָּה רְאוּיָה לִדָּבֵק בָּהּ כְּאֻמָּה זוֹ (יבמות עט א):


**Until the rains fell on them.** During the rainy season in Tishri. Their bones were not removed for burial. But is it not written, "His body should not remain [hanging] overnight"¹⁸? However they reasoned that it is better that one of the letters of the Torah be uprooted in order that God's Name be publicly sanctified. Passersby would ask "Why have these people come to this fate?" They were told [in response], "They are royal sons." "What did they do [wrong]?" "They assaulted downtrodden foreigners." [The passersby] would say, "There is no people better to associate with then this people."¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ Devorim 21:22. Why did Dovid let this happen?
¹⁹ Yevomos 79a.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:12:1)


**וַיִּקַּח אֶת עַצְמוֹת שָׁאוּל.** שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר לוֹ עֲלֵיהֶם ׳אֶל שָׁאוּל׳, שֶׁלֹּא נִסְפַּד כַּהֲלָכָה:


**He took Shaul's bones.** He [Dovid] was told concerning them, "Because of Shaul"²⁰ who was not eulogized according to halachah.

footnotes:
²⁰ Earlier in 21:1.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:13:1)


**וַיַּאַסְפוּ אֶת עַצְמוֹת.** שִׁבְעָה הַמּוּקָעִין:


**They gathered the bones.** Of the seven people that were hung there.²¹

footnotes:
²¹ In verse 9.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:14:1)


**כָּל אֲשֶׁר צִוָּה הַמֶּלֶךְ.** לְהַסְפִּיד עֲלֵיהֶם בְּכָל עָרֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**Everything that the king had commanded.** To eulogize them in all of the cities of Yisroel.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:16:1)


**וּמִשְׁקַל קֵינוֹ.** הוּא כְּמוֹ תִּיק שֶׁבְּרֹאשׁ בֵּית יָד הָרוֹמַח שֶׁקּוֹרִין ארישטוי״ל בלע״ז, וְעָשׂוּי כְּכוֹבַע, וְיֵשׁ דּוּגְמָתוֹ בִּזְבָחִים (פח ב, עיין שיטה מקובצת שם): רִמּוֹנֵי הַמְּעִיל לְמָה הֵם דּוֹמִין, כְּמִין קוֹלָסוֹת שֶׁבְּרָאשֵׁי הַתִּינוֹקוֹת, ׳קוֹלָסוֹת׳ היילמ״ש בלע״ז:


**A spear case weighing.** It is like a case that is at the top of spear's handle that is called aristuille in O.F., and it is made like a hat There is something comparable to it in Maseches Zevochim, "What were the pomegranates of the tunic²² similar to? They were like the caps on babies' heads" The caps [are called] hemles in O.F.

footnotes:
²² Zevachim 88b. The tunic worn by the Kohein Godol.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:16:2)


**וְהוּא חָגוּר.** חֲגוֹרָה חֲדָשָׁה. וְשָׁמַעְתִּי, שֶׁאוֹתוֹ הַיּוֹם נִתְחַנֵּךְ לִלְבּוֹשׁ כְּלֵי מִלְחָמָה, וְדֶרֶךְ הַמְחֻנָּכִים לַעֲשׂוֹת נִצָּחוֹן וּגְבוּרָה בְּיוֹם חִנּוּכָם, לִקְנוֹת שֵׁם, לְכָךְ וַיֹּאמֶר לְהַכּוֹת אֶת דָּוִד:


**Who was armed.** Armed with a new sword. I heard [an explanation] that on that day he was intitiated into the wearing of battle dress and it is the manner of those so initiated to [try to] be successful and mighty on the day of their initiation to make a name for themselves. That is why, **he said he was going to kill Dovid.**



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:17:1)


**וַיַּעֲזָר לוֹ.** שֶׁעֲזָרוֹ בִּתְפִלָּה, כִּדְאִיתָא בְּחֵלֶק (סנהדרין צה א):


**[Came] to his aid.** He helped him with his prayer as is brought in [chapter] Cheilek.²³

footnotes:
²³ Sanhedrin 95a.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:18:1)


**וַתְּהִי עוֹד הַמִּלְחָמָה.** כְּבָר קָדְמָה זוֹ לְכֻלָּן, וְלֹא מְנָאָה כָּאן, אֶלָּא לְצָרֵף מִיתַת אַרְבָּעָה בְּנֵי עָרְפָה:


**There was more war.** This [war] came before all [those mentioned here] and is only recounted here to combine the deaths of the four sons of Orpoh.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:19:1)


**אֶלְחָנָן.** דָּוִד:


**Elchonon.** Dovid.²⁴

footnotes:
²⁴ We know that it was Dovid who killed Golias in Shmuel I, 17:51, consequently this Elchonon must be Dovid.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:19:2)


**בֶּן יַעְרֵי אֹרְגִים.** שֶׁהָיוּ מִשְׁפַּחְתּוֹ אוֹרְגִים פָּרֹכֶת לַמִּקְדָּשׁ הַקָּרוּי יַעַר:


**The son of Yari [whose family] were weavers.** His family weaved the partition curtain of the Beis Hamikdosh that is [also] called a forest.²⁵

footnotes:
²⁵ The Beis Hamikdosh is called a forest because it was made with planks of acacia wood, see Shmos 26:15.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:19:3)


**כִּמְנוֹר אֹרְגִים.** (תרגום:) ״כְּאַכְסָן דְּגַרְדָאִין״, הַקָּרוּי אינשובל״א בלע״ז, תָּבֹא אֲרִיגָה וְתַעֲמֹד כְּנֶגֶד אֲרִיגָה:


**Like that of the weaver's rod.** [Yonoson translates:] "Like the weaver's rod," that is called "ensouple" in O.F. Let a weaver come and stand up against a weaver['s instrument].



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:20:1)


**אִישׁ מָדוֹן.** כְּתַרְגּוּמוֹ: ״גְּבַר דְּמִשְׁחָן״. שֶׁאֵין עִקַּר הַתֵּבָה אֶלָּא מ״ם וְדָלֶ״ת, כְּמוֹ מ״שְּׁאוֹן״ (תהלים סה:ח), שִׁי״ן וְאָלֶ״ף: וּמ״הֲמוֹן״(בראשית יז:ד), הֵ״א וּמֵ״ם. וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (דברי הימים א יא:כג) כְּתִיב: ״אִישׁ מִדָּה״, כְּלוֹמַר גָּבוֹהַּ מְאֹד, שֶׁאוֹמְדִין כַּמָּה מִדָּתוֹ:


**A giant man.** As [Yonoson] translated, "A man of [great] measure," the root [portion] of the word are the letters מ and ד.²⁶ We find [similarly] from the word שאון²⁷ [roar] the root letters are ש and א and from the word הָמוֹן²⁸ the root letters are ה and מ.²⁹ In Divrei Hayomim³⁰ it is written, "A man of measure"³¹ i.e., a very tall man who had people measuring his height.

footnotes:
²⁶ The ין being a suffix.
²⁷ Tehilim 65:8.
²⁸ Bereishis 17:4.
²⁹ These two words also having ין as a suffix.
³⁰ Divrei Hayomim I, 11:23.
³¹ Concerning this same giant refered to in our verse but there the word מִדָּה is used without the suffix ין.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 21:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/21:20:2)


**שֵׁשׁ וָשֵׁשׁ וְגוֹ׳.** פֵּרְשׁוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ בִּבְכוֹרוֹת (מה ב) שֶׁהֻצְרַךְ לוֹמַר עֶשְׂרִים וְאַרְבַּע, שֶׁלֹּא תֹּאמַר אֶצְבְּעוֹת שְׁתֵּי יָדָיו אֵינָן אֶלָּא שֵׁשׁ, וְאֶצְבְּעוֹת רַגְלָיו אֵינָן אֶלָּא שֵׁשׁ, לְכָךְ נֶאֱמַר עֶשְׂרִים וְאַרְבַּע, וְאִם כָּתַב עֶשְׂרִים וְאַרְבַּע וְלֹא אָמַר שֵׁשׁ וָשֵׁשׁ, הָיִיתִי אוֹמֵר שִׁבְעָה בְּאַחַת, וַחֲמִשָּׁה בְּאַחַת, וּמַהוּ ׳מִסְפָּר׳, שֶׁכֻּלָּן הָיוּ נִסְפָּרוֹת בְּסֵדֶר הָאֶצְבָּעוֹת בְּגַב הַיָּד:


**Numbered six each etc.** Our Rabbis explained in Maseches Bechoros³² that the verse had to tell us "twenty-four"³³ in order that you should not say that the fingers of his two hands only came to a total of six and that the toes of his [two] feet only came to a total of six that is why it is written, "twenty-four." If it had written in our verse only 'twenty-four" and not had said "six each," I would have [mistakeingly] said, there were seven on one [hand or foot] and five on the other. And what is the verse trying to indicate by the word "number"? That all these digital extremities could be counted in the normal arrangement of fingers on a hand.

footnotes:
³³ Despite the fact that given that his two hands and two feet had six digital extremities each, which obviously totals twenty-four.



## Chapter 22


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:1:1)


**בְּיוֹם הִצִּיל ה׳ אוֹתוֹ.** לְעֵת זִקְנָתוֹ, לְאַחַר שֶׁעָבְרוּ עָלָיו כָּל צָרוֹתָיו וְנִצּוֹל מִכֻּלָּם:


**On the day Adonoy saved him.** In his old age after all his troubles had passed and he had survived all of them.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:1:2)


**וּמִכַּף שָׁאוּל.** וַהֲלֹא שָׁאוּל בַּכְּלָל הָיָה, אֶלָּא שֶׁהָיָה אוֹיְבוֹ וְרוֹדְפוֹ מִכֻּלָּם, כַּיּוֹצֵא בּוֹ (שמואל ב ב:ל): ״תִּשְׁעָה עָשָׂר אִישׁ וַעֲשָׂהאֵל״, כַּיּוֹצֵא בּוֹ (יהושע ב:א): ״לְכוּ רְאוּ אֶת הָאָרֶץ וְאֶת יְרִיחוֹ״ וְכַיּוֹצֵא בּוֹ (מלכים א יא:א): ״וְהַמֶּלֶךְ שְׁלֹמֹה אָהַב נָשִׁים נָכְרִיּוֹת וְאֶת בַּת פַּרְעֹה״:


**And from the hands of Shaul.** Shaul was part of the general grouping [of "his enemies"]?¹ but he was his greatest enemy and pursuer. Similarly [we find], "Nineteen men and Asohel² Similarly, "Go spy out the land and Yericho."³ And similarly, "King Shlomo loved [many] foreign women and [he loved] the daughter of Pharoah."⁴

footnotes:
¹ I.e. Why is he being singled out?
² Earlier in 2:30. where Asohel is singled out because he was equal to all of them as Rashi points out there.
³ Yehoshua 2:1.
⁴ Melochim I, 11:1.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:2:1)


**סַלְעִי וּמְצוּדָתִי.** לְשׁוֹן חוֹזֶק הֵם, ׳סֶלַע׳ כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ, ׳מְצוּדָה׳ הִיא מְצוּדַת יְעָרִים, שֶׁקּוֹרִין פליישאי״ן בלע״ז, וְעַל שֵׁם הַנֵּס שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה לוֹ בְּסֶלַע הַמַּחְלוֹקֶת (שמואל א כג:כח), וְהַמְּצוּדוֹת בַּחוֹרְשָׁה:


**My Rock, my Fortress.** They are words denoting strength. Rock is obvious in its connotation [of strengh]. Fortress is [a reference to] a fortress amongst the forests that is called flassant in O.F. It is [also] a reference to the miracle that occurred by the rock at the crossroads⁵ and the fortresses of the forest.⁶

footnotes:
⁵ Shmuel I, 23:28.
⁶ Ibid, 23:19. Dovid’s location was betrayed to Shaul but eventually when Shaul reached the crossroads, he chose not to chase after Dovid.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:2:2)


**וּמְפַלְּטִי לִי.** מְפַלְּטִי עִם צְבָא יִשְׂרָאֵל בַּמִּלְחָמָה, וּפְעָמִים שֶׁהוּא מְפַלֵּט לִי כְּשֶׁאֲנִי לְבַדִּי, כְּגוֹן מִן יִשְׁבִּי בְּנוֹב (שמואל ב כא:טז):


**My Rescuer.** He saved me when I was with Yisroel's army at war and other times He saved me when I was alone⁷ for example [He saved me] from (the inhabitants of Nov)[“Yishbi-Benov”].

footnotes:
⁷ Rashi is explaining why Dovid in effect, says “me” twice. מְפַלְטי means “My Rescuer” and לי means “me”.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:1)


**צוּרִי.** לְשׁוֹן סֶלַע, שֶׁהַסֶּלַע מַחֲסֶה לְעוֹבְרֵי דֶרֶךְ, מִן הַמָּטָר וְהָרוּחוֹת, אבריאמ״ש בלע״ז:


**My Rock.** Language denoting a rock because a rock protects passerby from the rain and from the winds. Abriments in O.F.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:2)


**אֶחֱסֶה.** לְשׁוֹן כִּסּוּי, שֶׁהָיִיתִי מִתְכַּסֶּה לְעֶזְרָה:


**I take refuge.** Language denoting a covering that I was able to be protectively covered.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:3)


**מִשְׂגַּבִּי.** סוֹמְכֵנִי:


**My Stronghold.** He supported me.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:3:4)


**וּמְנוּסִי.** שֶׁהָיִיתִי נָס אֵלָיו לְעֶזְרָה:


**My Refuge.** I would run to him for protection.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:4:1)


**מְהֻלָּל אֶקְרָא ה׳.** כְּתַרְגּוּמוֹ; בְּקָרְאִי לוֹ אֲהוֹדֶנּוּ, לְפִי כִּי מֵאוֹיְבַי אֲנִי בּוֹטֵחַ שֶׁאִוָּשַׁע. וְיִתָּכֵן לִפְתּוֹר אֶקְרָא וְאִוָּשַׁע, לְשׁוֹן ׳הוֹוֶה׳:


**I call to Adonoy in praise.** As it is translated, when I called Him, I praised Him (with my mouth) because I trusted that I would be saved from my enemies. And it is possible to interpret the words אֶקְרָא and אִוׇּשֵׁעַ as being used in the present tense.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:5:1)


**אֲפָפוּנִי.** הִקִּיפוּנִי:


**Surrounded me.** Surrounded me.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:5:2)


**מִשְׁבְּרֵי מָוֶת.** כְּתַרְגּוּמוֹ: ״כְּאִתָּא דְּיָתְבָא עַל מַתְבְּרָא״, כֵּן שֵׁם מוֹשַׁב הָאֲבָנִים שֶׁהָאִשָּׁה יוֹלֶדֶת שָׁם:


**Death's destruction.** As [Yonoson] translates, "Like a woman sitting on the birthing chair," that is the name of the stone seat where women give birth.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ It’s name in hebrew is מַשְׁבֵּר.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:5:3)


**נַחֲלֵי.** גְּיָסוֹת שׁוֹטְפוֹת כְּנַחַל:


**The deluge.** Soldiers pouring forth like a stream.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:6:1)


**חֶבְלֵי.** כְּתַרְגּוּמוֹ: ״מַשְׁרִית״, כְּמוֹ (שמואל א י:ה): ״חֶבֶל נְבִיאִים״:


**The agony.** As [Yonoson] translates, "Groups," as [we find], "Group of prophets."⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Shmuel I, 10:5.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:6:2)


**קִדְּמוּנִי.** בָּאוּ לְפָנַי:


**Were before me.** Came before me.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:7:1)


**בַּצַּר לִי אֶקְרָא ה׳ וַיִּשְׁמַע וְגוֹ׳.** כָּךְ דֶּרֶךְ לְשׁוֹן הוֹוֶה, מְדַבֵּר לְשׁוֹן עָבָר וּלְשׁוֹן עָתִיד בְּפַעַם אַחַת:


**In my distress I called to God… He hears.** This is typical of the present tense it uses both past and future at the same time.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ אֶקְרָא is in past tense and וַיִשְׁמַע is in future tense.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:8:1)


**וַיִּתְגָּעַשׁ וַתִּרְעַשׁ.** לֹא עַל נִסִּים שֶׁאֵרְעוּהוּ נֶאֱמַר, אֶלָּא עַל נִסִּים שֶׁנַּעֲשׂוּ לְיִשְׂרָאֵל, וְרֹאשׁ הַמִּקְרָא מְחֻבָּר עַל סוֹפוֹ:


**Shook and shuddered.** This is not referring to the miracles that happened to him but to the miracles that happened to Yisroel and the beginning of the verse is connected to the end.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:8:2)


**כִּי חָרָה לוֹ.** ׳כִּי׳ מְשַׁמֵּשׁ כָּאן בִּלְשׁוֹן ׳כַּאֲשֶׁר׳, וְכֵן פִּתְרוֹנוֹ, וּכְשֶׁחָרָה לוֹ מִפְּנֵי מַכְעִיסָיו, נִתְגַּעֲשָׁה וְנִתְרַעֲשָׁה הָאָרֶץ, וּמוֹסְדוֹת הַשָּׁמַיִם רָגְזוּ וְרָעֲשׁוּ:


**When God became angry.** The word כי is being used here to mean "when," and this is its interpretation, when God became angry on account of his antagonists the world shook and shuddered and the pillars of heaven trembled and shook.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:9:1)


**עָלָה עָשָׁן בְּאַפּוֹ.** כֵּן דֶּרֶךְ הַכּוֹעֵס, יוֹצֵא עָשָׁן מִנְּחִירָיו, וְכֵן (תהלים יח:ט): ״עָלָה עָשָׁן בְּאַפּוֹ״. וְזֶהוּ כָּל לְשׁוֹן חֲרוֹן אַף, שֶׁהָאַף נוֹחֵר וּמַעֲלֶה הֶבֶל:


**Smoke rose from His Nostrils.** Such is the manner of an angry person, smoke comes out of his nostrils and so [we find], "Smoke has risen from My nostrils."¹¹ This is always the meaning of the words "Anger of the nose," the nose flares and lets out air.

footnotes:
¹¹ Yeshayohu 65:5.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:9:2)


**וְאֵשׁ מִפִּיו תֹּאכֵל.** מִגִּזְרַת דְּבַר פִּיו, תֹּאכַל אֵשׁ בָּרְשָׁעִים:


**Fire from His Mouth consumed [the wicked].** Through the decree utterred from His Mouth fire [came forth] and consumed the wicked.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:10:1)


**וַיֵּט שָׁמַיִם.** לְהִנָּקֵם מֵאוֹיְבָיו, מִמִּצְרַיִם וּמִפַּרְעֹה:


**He bent heaven.** To take revenge from His enemies, from Mitzrayim and from Pharoah.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:12:1)


**וַיָּשֶׁת חֹשֶׁךְ סְבִיבוֹתָיו.** לְסֻכָּה, כָּעִנְיָן שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (שמות יד:כ): ״וַיְהִי הֶעָנָן וְהַחֹשֶׁךְ״, מַפְסִיק בֵּין מִצְרַיִם לְיִשְׂרָאֵל:


**He put [a protective] darkness around.** For [protective] cover as it says, "The pillar of cloud and the darkness separated between Mitzrayim and Yisroel."¹²

footnotes:
¹² See Shemos 14:20.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:12:2)


**חַשְׁרַת מַיִם עָבֵי שְׁחָקִים.** מֵאַיִן הָיָה הַחֹשֶׁךְ, עָבֵי שְׁחָקִים הָיוּ, שֶׁהֵן חוֹשְׁרִין מַיִם עַל הָאָרֶץ:


**Abundant waters, thick clouds of the sky.** From where did the darkness come? They came from thick clouds that sprinkle water on the earth.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:12:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:12:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:12:3)


**חַשְׁרַת.** לְשׁוֹן כְּבָרָה הוּא, שֶׁהוּא נוֹפֵל עַל הָאָרֶץ דַּק דַּק (תענית ט ב), וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר בְּאַגָּדוֹת הַרְבֵּה: חוֹשְׁרִין אוֹתוֹ בִּכְבָרָה. וְיֵשׁ לִפְתּוֹר ׳חַשְׁרַת׳, לְשׁוֹן ׳קֶשֶׁר׳, שֶׁמִּתְקַשְּׁרִין הַשָּׁמַיִם בְּעָבִים עַל יְדֵי הַמַּיִם, כְּמוֹ ׳וְחִשּׁוּרֵיהֶם׳ הָאָמוּר בְּאוֹפַנֵּי הַמְּכוֹנָה (מלכים א ז:לג), שֶׁהֵם זְרוֹעוֹת הָעֵץ הַמְהַדְּקִין וְקוֹשְׁרִין עִגּוּלֶיהָ יַחַד:


**Sprinkled.** [This is] language that denotes a sieve, [the water] falls to the earth in small droplets. This [same language] is used in many places in Aggadah, "They dice it in a sieve," Some interpret חַשְׁרַת as denoting a binding, that the skies became bound up with clouds because of the water [in them] as [the word] "the spokes"¹³ used [in connection with] the wheels of a wagon. They are wooden spokes that bond and bind the wheel's perimeter together.

footnotes:
¹³ Melochim I, 7:33.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:13:1)


**מִנֹּגַהּ נֶגְדּוֹ.** שֶׁלֹּא תֹאמַר בַּחֹשֶׁךְ הוּא שָׁרוּי, אֶלָּא הַנֹּגַהּ לִפְנִים מִן הַמְּחִיצָה, וּמֵאוֹתוֹ נֹגַהּ אֲשֶׁר לְפָנָיו, בָּעֲרוּ גַּחֲלֵי אֵשׁ, שֶׁנִּשְׁתַּלְּחוּ חִצִּים עַל מִצְרַיִם:


**From the brightness before Him.** You should not conclude that He dwells in darkness¹⁴ but rather it is light that is within the boundary and it is from that light that is before Him that the burning coals of fire [came forth]. [It is from there] that the arrows were shot at Mitzrayim.

footnotes:
¹⁴ I.e., because the previous verse mentioned darkness in connection with God. Furthermore, the verse here is describing punishment meted out to Yisroel’s enemies. The verse is telling us that the punishment’s source is light.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:16:1)


**יִגָּלוּ מוֹסְדוֹת תֵּבֵל.** שֶׁנִּבְקַע הַתְּהוֹם, כְּשֶׁנִּבְקַע יַם סוּף נִבְקְעוּ כָּל מֵימוֹת שֶׁבָּעוֹלָם (ילקוט שמעוני רמז קסא):


**The foundations of the earth were revealed.** The deep seas split open. When the Reed Sea split all the world's waters split [as well].


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:16:2)


**מִנִּשְׁמַת.** מִכֹּחַ נְשִׁיבַת רוּחַ אַפּוֹ:


**By the wind.** From the force of wind blowing from His Nostrils.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:18:1)


**כִּי אָמְצוּ.** כַּאֲשֶׁר אָמְצוּ:


**They were too powerful.** When they were overpowering.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:21:1)


**כְּצִדְקָתִי.** כְּשֶׁיָּצְאוּ אַחֲרָיו לַמִּדְבָּר וְסָמְכוּ עַל הַבְטָחָתוֹ:


**For my rightesnous.** When they [the people of Yisroel] followed God into the desert and had faith in His promise.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:26:1)


**עִם חָסִיד תָּמִים נְבָר.** כְּנֶגֶד שְׁלֹשָׁה אָבוֹת, שֶׁשִּׁלֵּם הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא גְּמוּל צִדְקָתָם לִבְנֵיהֶם:


**To the Kind, Sincere, Pure.** [These three nouns] represent the three Patriarchs. God paid the reward of their righteousness to their children.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ Kind refers to Avrohom, sincere refers to Yitchok and pure refers to Yakov.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:27:1)


**וְעִם עִקֵּשׁ.** פַּרְעֹה:


**And to those who are crooked.** [This refers to] Paroh


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:27:2)


**תִּתְפַּל.** לְשׁוֹן נִפְתָּל וְעִקֵּשׁ, וּבְסֵפֶר תְּהִלִּים (תהלים יח:כז) כָּתוּב: ״תִּתְפַּתָּל״. דָּבָר אַחֵר: **יִשְׁלַח מִמָּרוֹם יִקָּחֵנִי.** עַל עַצְמוֹ אָמַר, כְּשֶׁהָיָה נֶחְפָּז לָלֶכֶת מִפְּנֵי שָׁאוּל בְּסֶלַע הַמַּחְלְקוֹת וְהָיָה קָרוֹב לְהִתָּפֵשׂ, וּמַלְאָךְ בָּא אֶל שָׁאוּל לֵאמֹר: מַהֲרָה וְלֵכָה כִּי פָשְׁטוּ פְלִשְׁתִּים: **וַיָּשֶׁב וְגוֹ׳ כְּצִדְקָתִי.** שֶׁלֹּא הֲרַגְתִּיו בְּכָרְתִי כְּנַף מְעִילוֹ:


**You act perversely.** [This is] language denoting jagged and crooked. In the book of Tehilim¹⁶ it is written, תִּתְפַּתָּל.¹⁷ An alternative explanation,¹⁸ [when writing] "He delivers [salvation] from on high,"¹⁹ [Dovid] was refering to himself.²⁰ When he was rushing to escape Shaul [and had reached] the rock at the crossroads,²¹ and was close to being captured an angel came to Shaul saying, "Go quickly because the Pelishtim have spread out [in battle formation over the terrain.]"²² "He rewarded me…according to my righteousness."²³ [Refering to the fact] that I did not kill him [Shaul] when I [instead just] cut off the corner of his coat.

footnotes:
¹⁶ Tehilim 18:27. Chapter 18 of Tehilim is a repetition of this song of Dovid almost verbatim.
¹⁷ Rashi is proving that the root of this word is פתל.
¹⁸ Of the previous verses starting from verse 17.
¹⁹ Earlier in our chapter, verse 17.
²⁰ Not to the people of Yisroel in general.
²¹ In Shmuel I, 23:26.
²² Ibid 23:27.
²³ Earlier in our chapter, verse 25.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:31:1)


**אִמְרַת ה׳ צְרוּפָה.** בְּרוּרָה, מַבְטִיחַ וְעוֹשֶׂה:


**The word of Adonoy is clear.** It is clear, He makes promises and keeps them.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:33:1)


**וַיַּתֵּר תָּמִים דַּרְכִּי.** מִכָּל מִכְשׁוֹל, וּמִכָּל חֵטְא, וּמִכָּל מְסֻכָּן, עַד כִּי הָיָה שָׁלֵם וְכָבוּשׁ:


**He cleared my path to perfection.** From any obstacle, from any sin and from any danger until there was peace and [the land was] conquered.



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:35:1)


**וְנִחַת קֶשֶׁת נְחוּשָׁה זְרֹעֹתָי.** וְנִדְרְכָה קֶשֶׁת נְחוּשָׁה עַל זְרוֹעִי, שֶׁיֵּשׁ בִּי כֹּחַ לְדָרְכָהּ. קְשָׁתוֹת הָיוּ תְּלוּיִם לְדָוִד בְּבֵיתוֹ, וְהָיוּ מַלְכֵי הָאֻמּוֹת בָּאִין וְרוֹאִין אוֹתָן, וְאוֹמְרִים זֶה לָזֶה: אַתָּה סָבוּר שֶׁהוּא יָכוֹל לְדָרְכָן, אֵין זֶה אֶלָּא לְיָרְאֵנוּ, וְדָוִד שׁוֹמֵעַ וְנוֹטְלָן וּמְכַתְּתָן לִפְנֵיהֶם. וּדְרִיכַת קֶשֶׁת לְשׁוֹן חַיִּת הִיא, וְכֵן ״תְּהִלִּים חִצֶּיךָ נִחְתּוּ בִי״:


**My arms to bend a copper bow.** So that a copper bow can be bent by my arms because I have the strength to bend them. Dovid had bows hanging in his palace, and kings of other nations would come and see them and ask each other, Do you think that he can bend them? This is only to scare us. Dovid would hear them and would take them [the bows] and bend them in front of them [the kings]. The binding of a bow is denoted by the word חִתַּת²⁴ and so [we find], "Your arrows have been shot into me".²⁵

footnotes:
²⁴ In our verse it says ונחת, חתת is it’s root.
²⁵ Tehilim 38:3.



### Verse 36

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:36:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:36:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:36:1)


**וְעַנְוָתְךָ תַּרְבֵּנִי.** הִגְדַּלְתָּ לִי מִדַּת עַנְוְתָנוּתְךָ:


**You have been abundant for my sake with Your humility.** You have been abundant for my sake, with Your humility.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:37:1)


**תַּרְחִיב צַעֲדִי.** כְּשֶׁאָדָם מְדַבֵּק רַגְלָיו זוֹ בָּזוֹ, הוּא נוֹחַ לִפּוֹל, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר (משלי ד:יב): ״בְּלֶכְתְּךָ לֹא יֵצַר צַעֲדֶךָ״:


**You have expanded my stride.** When a man keeps his legs together, it is easier for him to fall, and so it says, "When you travel, do not narrow your stride."²⁶

footnotes:
²⁶ Mishlei 4:12.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:37:2)


**קַרְסֻלָּי.** עֲקֵבַי:


**My ankles.** My ankles.



### Verse 42

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:42:1)


**יִשְׁעוּ וְאֵין מוֹשִׁיעַ וְגוֹ׳.** הֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא מְסֹרָס, יִשְׁעוּ אֶל ה׳ וְלֹא עָנָם, וְאֵין מוֹשִׁיעַ, כְּמוֹ (ישעיהו יז:ז): ״יִשְׁעֶה הָאָדָם אֶל עֹשֵׂהוּ״, כְּמוֹ יִפְנֶה. וּמְנַחֵם חִבְּרוֹ עִם (בראשית ד:ד): ״וַיִּשַׁע ה׳ אֶל הֶבֶל״, וּפָתַר בּוֹ לְשׁוֹן עֲתִירָה, וְנוֹפֵל הַלָּשׁוֹן עַל הַמַּעְתִּיר וְעַל הַנֶּעְתָּר, כְּמוֹ (בראשית כה:כא): ״וַיֶּעְתַּר יִצְחָק לַה׳״, ״וַיֵּעָתֶר לוֹ ה׳״, אַף כָּאן ״יִשְׁעוּ וְאֵין מוֹשִׁיעַ״ נוֹפֵל עַל הַמִּתְפַּלֵּל, וַיִּשַׁע ה׳ נוֹפֵל עַל הַלָּשׁוֹן, עַל מִי שֶׁמִּתְפַּלְּלִין לְפָנָיו:


**They cried out there was no one to deliver them.** The words of verse are not written in order [the proper order should be] "They will cry out to Adonoy but He will not answer them and no one will deliver them"²⁷ as [we find], "Man will turn to his Maker",²⁸ as if to say "turn".²⁹ Menachem connected [the word יִשְׁעֶה] with [the verse,] "Adonoy listened to Hevel",³⁰ and he [Menachem] interpreted it as "listening." This word can be used to denote both the listener and the listened to as [we find], "Yitzchok implored Adonoy… and Adonoy listened to him."³¹ So too here "They will implore [God] and they will not be saved"³² is a case where the word יִשְׁעוּ is refering to the one praying and "Adonoy listened…"³³ is a case where the word is refering to the [Holy] One being prayed to.

footnotes:
²⁷ The two words ואין מושיע need to be removed from their position at the beginning of the verse and placed instead at the end of the verse as its last two words.
²⁸ Yeshayohu 17:7.
²⁹ i.e., ישעה means “turn to.”
³⁰ Bereishis 4:4.
³¹ Bereishis 25:21.
³² In our verse.
³³ By Hevel’s offering.



### Verse 43

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:43:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:43:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:43:1)


**אֶרְקָעֵם.** אֶרְמְסֵם, וְהַרְבֵּה יֵשׁ בְּסֵפֶר יְחֶזְקֵאל: (ו:יא): ״וּרְקַע בְּרַגְלְךָ״ (כה:ו): ״וְרַקְעֲךָ בְּרָגֶל״:


**I will trample them.** I will trample them. There are many cases like this in the book of Yechezkiel, [such as] "You will trample with your feet"³⁴ [and] "You trampled with [your] foot."³⁵

footnotes:
³⁴ Yechezkiel 6:11.
³⁵ Ibid, 25:6.



### Verse 44

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:44:1)


**מֵרִיבֵי.** מִדּוֹאֵג, מֵאֲחִיתֹפֶל, וְשָׁאוּל, וְהַזִּיפִים:


**Antagonists.** From Doeg, Achitophel, Shaul and the Ziffites.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:44:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:44:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:44:2)


**תִּשְׁמְרֵנִי לְרֹאשׁ גּוֹיִם.** צְפַנְתַּנִי לְכָךְ. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה; אָמַר דָּוִד: רִבּוֹנוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם, הַצִּילֵנִי מִדִּינוֹ שֶׁל יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁאִם אַטְהוּ אוֹ אֶרְדֶּה אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל בַּעֲבוֹדָתוֹ, אֲנִי נֶעֱנָשׁ, אֶלָּא לְרֹאשׁ הַפְּלִשְׁתִּים תְּשִׂימֵנִי, וְהֵם יַעַבְדוּנִי, וַעֲלֵיהֶם לֹא אֶעֱנָשׁ:


**You preserved me to be a leader of nations.** He kept me hidden for this purpose. The Midrash Aggadah [interprets our verse], "Dovid said, "Master of the universe, spare me from [potential] judgement concerning [the people of] Yisroel for if I shall cause [Yisroel] to turn away [from You] or I rule them harshly during the course of my service I will be punished [by You]. Rather, make me the leader of the Pelishtim and let them serve me [because] I will not be punished on their account.



### Verse 45

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:45:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:45:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:45:1)


**יִתְכַּחֲשׁוּ לִי.** מֵחֲמַת יִרְאָה, יֹאמְרוּ לִי כְּזָבִים:


**Lied to me.** Out of fear they told me lies.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:45:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:45:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:45:2)


**לִשְׁמוֹעַ אֹזֶן יִשָּׁמְעוּ לִי.** אֲפִלּוּ שֶׁלֹּא בְּפָנַי, יָגוּרוּ מִפָּנַי, לְמִשְׁמַע אָזְנֵיהֶם לָסוּר אֶל מִשְׁמַעְתִּי:


**[As soon] as their ears hear [my wish] they listen to [and obey] me.** Even when they are not in my presence they are fearful of me [to the point that as soon] as their ears hear [my wish], they move [to obey] my command.



### Verse 46

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:46:1)


**בְּנֵי נֵכָר יִבֹּלוּ.** יִלְאוּ, לְשׁוֹן (ירמיהו ח:יג): ״וְהֶעָלֶה נָבֵל״ פלישטירונ״ט בלע״ז:


**Foreign people will wither.** Will wither [Similar to] the language of "I will become withered".³⁶ Pagisterant in O.F.

footnotes:
³⁶ Yirmiyahu 8:11.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:46:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:46:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:46:2)


**וְיַחְגְּרוּ.** לְשׁוֹן פִּסְחִים:


**They will become lame.** Lame people.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:46:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:46:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:46:3)


**מִמִּסְגְּרוֹתָם.** מִקֹּשִׁי יִסּוּרֵי מַסְגֵּר שֶׁאֲנִי מְיַסְּרָן בָּהֶם:


**From [the agony of] their imprisonment.** From the tortures of imprisonment that I will inflict upon them.



### Verse 47

###### Rashi on II Samuel 22:47:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:47:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/22:47:1)


**חַי ה׳.** הָעוֹשֶׂה לִי אֵלֶּה:


**Adonoy lives.** Who does all of this for me.



## Chapter 23


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:1:1)


**וְאֵלֶּה דִּבְרֵי דָוִד.** נְבוּאַת דָּוִד אַחֲרוֹנִים, וּמִי הֵם הָרִאשׁוֹנִים, דִּבְרֵי הַשִּׁירָה הָאֲמוּרִים לְמַעְלָה, אֲבָל בְּכָל שִׁירוֹת וְתִשְׁבָּחוֹת שֶׁאָמַר, אֵין נִקְרָאִים דְּבָרִים:


**These are Dovid's words.** Dovid's last prophecy. What were the first words? The words of the song that were said above.¹ But all the songs and praises that he [Dovid] said are not called "words".²

footnotes:
² The use of the word דִּבְרֵי here denotes prophecy, not songs and praises.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:1:2)


**הוּקַם עָל.** הוּקַם לְמַעְלָה:


**Raised on high.** Raised from Above.³

footnotes:
³ The word עָל, is used to denote לְמַעְלָה, “Above.”


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:1:3)


**וּנְעִים זְמִירוֹת יִשְׂרָאֵל.** אֵין יִשְׂרָאֵל מְשׁוֹרְרִים בַּמִּקְדָּשׁ, אֶלָּא שִׁירוֹתָיו וּזְמִירוֹתָיו:


**Sweet songs for Yisroel.** Yisroel does not sing any songs in the Beis Hamikdosh other then his [Dovid's] songs and hymns.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:2:1)


**דִּבֶּר בִּי.** הִשְׁרָה בִּי רוּחַ קָדְשׁוֹ וְנִדְבַּר בִּי, וְכָל לְשׁוֹן נְבוּאָה, נוֹפֵל בּוֹ לְשׁוֹן דִּבּוּר, כְּמוֹ (במדבר יב:ב): ״הֲרַק אַךְ בְּמֹשֶׁה דִּבֶּר הֲלֹא גַּם בָּנוּ דִּבֶּר״ (במדבר יב:ו): ״אֲדַבֶּר בּוֹ״, וְטַעְמוֹ שֶׁל דָּבָר לְפִי שֶׁהָרוּחַ נִכְנָס בּוֹ בְּקֶרֶב הַנָּבִיא, וּמְדַבֵּר בּוֹ:


**Speaks through me.** He bestowed His holy spirit on me and spoke through me. Whenever [the verse refers] to prophecy, the word דִּבֵּר is appropriate as [we find] "Did [Adonoy] only speak to Moshe, He also spoke to us."⁴ "I will speak through him."⁵ The reason for it is because the spirit enters into the prophet and speaks through him.

footnotes:
⁴ Bamidbar 12:2.
⁵ Ibid 12:6.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:3:1)


**לִי דִּבֶּר צוּר יִשְׂרָאֵל.** אֵלַי דִּבֶּר וְצִוָּה צוּר יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁאֶהְיֶה מוֹשֵׁל בָּאָדָם, בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁנִּקְרְאוּ אָדָם, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (יחזקאל לד:לא): ״אָדָם אַתֶּם״ (יבמות סא א), וְאֶהְיֶה צַדִּיק מוֹשֵׁל וִירֵא אֱלֹהִים. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ (מועד קטן טז ב) פֵּרְשׁוּהוּ בְּלָשׁוֹן אַחֵר: אָמַר דָּוִד: אֱלֹהֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל לִי דִּבֶּר, אֵלַי דִּבֶּר צוּר יִשְׂרָאֵל, מוֹשֵׁל בָּאָדָם אֲנִי, וּמִי מוֹשֵׁל בִּי, צַדִּיק, שֶׁאֲנִי גּוֹזֵר גְּזֵרָה וְהוּא מְבַטְּלָהּ. אֲבָל לְפִי יִשּׁוּב הַמִּקְרָאוֹת, הָרִאשׁוֹן הוּא פְּשׁוּטוֹ שֶׁל מִקְרָא:


**The Rock of Yisroel spoke to me.** The Rock of Yisroel spoke to and commanded me that I should be a ruler over men, over Yisroel who are called "men" as it is written, "You are men." [He also told me] that I will be a righteous person, a ruler and a God fearing person. Our Rabbis interpreted [this verse] with a different explanation:⁶ Dovid said [as follows:] "The God of Yisroel spoke to me, the Rock of Yisroel spoke to me, I am ruler over men. And who rules over me? The righteous man, because I [may] issue a decree and he [can] annul it." But according to the flow of these verses it is the first explanation that is the simple interpretation of these verses.

footnotes:
⁶ Moed Koton 16b.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:4:1)


**וּכְאוֹר בֹּקֶר יִזְרַח שָׁמֶשׁ.** וְהִבְטִיחֵנִי שֶׁתֵּלֵךְ גְּדֻלָּתִי הָלוֹךְ וְגָדֵל, כְּאוֹר נֹגַהּ אֲשֶׁר הוֹלֵךְ וָאוֹר:


**As day breaks with the sun shining.** He promised me that my glory will continuously grow like the light at day break which gets brighter and brighter.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:4:2)


**בֹּקֶר לֹא עָבוֹת.** אוֹר שֶׁאֵינוֹ אָפֵל:


**A cloudless morning.** A light that does not dim.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:4:3)


**מִנֹּגַהּ מִמָּטָר דֶּשֶׁא מֵאָרֶץ.** מֵאִיר בָּקְרִי יוֹתֵר מִנֹּגַהּ הַבָּא מִמָּטָר, כְּחֹם צַח עֲלֵי אוֹר, כְּשֶׁהַמָּטָר יוֹרֵד עַל הָאָרֶץ מְלֵאָה דְשָׁאִים, וְהַשֶּׁמֶשׁ זוֹרֵחַ עָלָיו וּמַבְהִיק, וְכֵן פִּתְרוֹנוֹ, יוֹתֵר מִנֹּגַהּ הַבָּא מִמָּטָר שֶׁל דֶּשֶׁא מֵאָרֶץ:


**[Brighter] then the light that comes with the rain that nourishes the land.** My morning is brighter then the light that comes with rain, which is a clear warmth [shining forth] rays of light. When the rain falls on vegetated earth and the sun shines on it and causes it to brighten. And this is how the verse is interpreted, More then the light that comes from rain [falling on] vegetated earth.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:1)


**כִּי לֹא כֵן בֵּיתִי עִם אֵל.** שֶׁיְּהֵא בָּקְרִי עָבוֹת:


**For is not my house with God.** That I should have a cloudy morning.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:2)


**כִּי בְרִית עוֹלָם.** הַתּוֹרָה אֲשֶׁר שָׂם לִי, עֲרוּכָה בְּכָל בֵּיתִי וּשְׁמוּרָה:


**For an eternal convenant.** [This refers to] the Torah that He placed for me, that He has arranged for all my household, and kept there.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:3)


**כִּי כָל יִשְׁעִי וְכָל חֵפֶץ.** צְרָכַי מוּכָנִים לְפָנָיו, וַהֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר:


**All my hopes and desires [are before God].** My needs are set before Him. This is a shortened verse.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ The verse itself mentions Dovid’s hopes and desires but does specify anything further about them.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:5:4)


**כִּי לֹא יַצְמִיחַ.** עוֹד לְמֶלֶךְ אַחַר מַלְכוּתִי. דָּבָר אַחֵר: **כִּי בְרִית עוֹלָם שָׂם לְבֵיתִי,** לִהְיוֹת מַלְכוּתִי קַיֶּמֶת, וְהַבְּרִית **עֲרוּכָה בַכֹּל,** בְּפִי כָּל הַנְּבִיאִים:


**Because [my kingdom] will never be outgrown.** There will be no other king after my kingdom.⁸ Another interpretation: He has made an eternal convenant for my House, that my kingdom shall be everlasting and this convenant is known to all, it is mentioned by all the prophets.

footnotes:
⁸ As God promised Dovid earlier in 7:13 that his kingdom will be eternal.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:6:1)


**כְּקוֹץ מֻנָּד.** קרדו״ן בלע״ז, שֶׁבְּקַטְנוּתוֹ רַךְ נָע וָנָד, וְסוֹפוֹ מִתְקַשֶּׁה, עַד אֲשֶׁר **לֹא בְיָד יִקָּחֻהוּ**:


**Like an itinerant thorn.** *Cordon* in O.F. When [this thorn is] young it is soft. It then travels to and fro and eventually hardens until it is [so sharp that it is] unable to be held in one's hand.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:7:1)


**וְאִישׁ יִגַּע בָּהֶם יִמָּלֵא.** צָרִיךְ שֶׁיִּלְבַּשׁ אֶת בְּשָׂרוֹ בַּרְזֶל, וִימַלֵּא יָדוֹ בִּכְלֵי זַיִן לְקַצְּצוֹ:


**Any man wanting to touch them should don.** He must cover his body with armor and arm himself with a weapon to cut it down


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:7:2)


**וּבָאֵשׁ שָׂרוֹף יִשָּׂרְפוּ בַּשָּׁבֶת.** וְאֵין שָׁם תַּקָּנָה אֶלָּא שְׂרֵפָה, וְלָשֶׁבֶת וּלְהִתְחַמֵּם כְּנֶגְדָּם, כָּךְ הָרְשָׁעִים אֵין לָהֶם תַּקָּנָה, אֶלָּא שְׂרֵפָה בְּגֵיהִנֹּם:


**They will be completely burnt totally consumed by fire.** There is no solution other then burning [these thorns] and to sit and warm oneself by the fire. So too, for the wicked there is no solution other then burning in Gehennom.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:7:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:7:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:7:3)


**בַּשָּׁבֶת.** הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא יוֹשֵׁב עַל כִּסֵּא הַדִּין:


**[While one] sits.** [A reference to] God sitting on the throne of Judgement.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:8:1)


**אֲשֶׁר לְדָוִד יוֹשֵׁב בַּשֶּׁבֶת.** יוֹשֵׁב בְּשֶׁבֶת סַנְהֶדְרִין, וְהוּא תַּחְכְּמוֹנִי, וְהוּא רֹאשׁ הַשָּׁלִישִׁי, אָב בָּנוּי בְּחָכְמָה וּבִגְבוּרָה, בִּשְׁלֹשָׁה דְּבָרִים הַלָּלוּ הוּא רֹאשׁ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (שמואל א טז:יח): ״וּנְבוֹן דָּבָר וְאִישׁ תֹּאַר וְגִבּוֹר חַיִל״:


**That were with Dovid, who sat in the council.** Who sat in the Sanhedrin Court. That is [alluded to by the word] wisdom. "He was a leader in three [distinct areas]," He was first in beauty, wisdom and strength. He was the leader in these three categories as it is written "[A man] of understanding of attractive build and a strong soldier."⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Shmuel I, 16:18. Rashi is not quoting this verse in its exact order.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:8:2)


**עֲדִינוֹ הָעֶצְנִי.** כְּשֶׁעוֹסֵק בַּתּוֹרָה, כּוֹרֵךְ וּמְקַשֵּׁר עַצְמוֹ כְּתוֹלַעַת, כְּמוֹ (איוב לח:לא): ״מַעֲדַנּוֹת כִּימָה״, וּכְשֶׁיּוֹצֵא לַמִּלְחָמָה, הָיָה קָשֶׁה כָּעֵץ, וְהוֹרֵג שְׁמוֹנֶה מֵאוֹת חָלָל בְּמִלְחָמָה אַחַת (מועד קטן טז ב):


**Adino the Etzonite.** When he was studying Torah he would fold and bind himself up like a worm¹⁰ as [we find, this word in the verse] "The bonds of Pleiades."¹¹ And when he went out to war he was as hard as wood¹² and would kill eight hundred men in one battle.¹³

footnotes:
¹⁰ Rashi is explaining the name עֲדִינוֹ as reffering to the word מַעֲדַנּוֹת which means bindings.
¹¹ Eyuv 38:31.
¹² Here Rashi is explaining the עֶצְנִי part of his name which contains within it the word עֵץ which means wood.
¹³ Tanchuma Masoei.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:9:1)


**בֶּן דּוֹדוֹ.** כָּךְ שְׁמוֹ:


**The son of Dodo.** This was his name.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:9:2)


**בִּשְׁלֹשָׁה הַגִּבֹּרִים.** הַמְעֻלִּים שֶׁבַּגִּבּוֹרִים:


**One of the three mighty men.** Of the best of the mighty men.¹⁴

footnotes:
¹⁴ There were more then just three men involved in fighting the Pelishtim.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:9:3)


**בְּחָרְפָם בַּפְּלִשְׁתִּים.** תָּקְעוּ כַּף לְהִלָּחֵם בָּהֶם וּלְנַצְּחָם:


**When they humiliated the Pelishtim.** They made an agreement with each other to fight them and to vanquish them.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:10:1)


**אַךְ לְפַשֵּׁט.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״לְחַלְּצָא קְטִילַיָּא״, לֹא הָיָה אִישׁ לְעָזְרוֹ לְהָרוֹג, אֶלָּא כֻּלָּם פּוֹשְׁטִים אַחֲרָיו אֶת הַחֲלָלִים:


**But only to strip [the corpses].** Yonoson translates: "To strip the dead." There was no one to help him kill [the Pelishtim] but they were all stripping the corpses after [he had killed them].



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:11:1)


**הֲרָרִי.** מִן הָהָר:


**From the mountain.** From the mountain.


###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:11:2)


**לַחַיָּה.** לִגְדוּד כְּחַיּוֹת הַשָּׂדֶה:


**For [war like] beast in the wild.** [Gathering] to become troops like the beasts gather in the wild.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Samuel/r/23:13:1)


**מֵהַשְּׁלֹשִׁים רֹאשׁ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: ״מִגִּבָּרֵי רֵישֵׁי מַשְׁרִיתָא״:


**Of the thirty leaders.** Yonoson translates: From the men who were leaders within the army.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Samuel 23:14:1
